X → ∞ · The Prime Treatise

Chapter IX → ∞ — one vision, many pods

Every crypto idea Saheb has written down since 2021 turns out to be one idea built several times, each time closer to the real thing. This chapter states that idea, shows how the pieces fit, and explains how the rest of the treatise is organized.

The vision in one paragraph

One account, one coin, one rail. A single prime coin, Rei (零), ticker XR, whose supply steps up through the prime numbers. A regulated rail, American Perpetual, that lists perpetual futures on everything through MIAX Futures, clears at OCC, and pays its owner 60% of the fees MIAX collects on every perpetual product. And around them, a set of pods: the desk that trades it, the surfaces people use it through, and the capital and relationships that move it. The variable changes from era to era (a currency, a fund, an exchange, a coin), but the shape stays the same: one core asset that the whole system is denominated in, with everything else built to make it worth more. That is what X → ∞ means here.

60%of the fees MIAX collects on every perpetual product, with no end dateMaster Perpetual Products License Agreement v2.8, final text of 30 September 2026.
2 → 3 → 5 → 7Rei's supply steps, one prime at a time1 Rei = 10¹⁰ XR; supply starts at 2 Rei and each step mints the gap to the next prime, first split by Riemann's R function ("prime rata"). See Chapter IX.

Sources: AP data room (30 Sep 2026); MPPLA v2.8 execution form; "Rei — The World a $100 Billion Coin Makes" (16 Oct 2026)

The shape that keeps coming back

Read in date order, the documents repeat three engines.

  1. A main coin that owns many product coins. In August 2022 a single currency (OHM) prices the whole "World of Ohm". By October 2022 a fixed-supply main token sits above a family of app tokens in ManifestOS. In 2023 the rule is written down: ecosystem coins must stay below the main coin, and the main coin should be about 100 million tokens priced in the tens of dollars. Coin Denominated Reality (Nov 2023) puts a main chain coin in a public company's treasury above 100+ dividend-paying product coins. Rei is where this line lands.
  2. A fund that wants to become a bank. Andromeda Capital Partners (2024) pools capital into options trades on ETH and SOL, then becomes ACM and the 3S Capital brochure, and names its end point as "the first legal crypto Prime bank in the world".
  3. An exchange where the house earns the fees. BTCZPERP on the Z stablecoin (2023), "Lend. Options. Perps." (2023), the Andromeda Terminal (2024), then Greyline → GLX → American Perpetual (Nov 2025 → Sep 2026): perpetual futures on US stocks, built with MIAX, ending as a licence that pays 60% of every perpetual's fees.

The October 2026 Rei documents join all three: the coin is the main coin; MIAX's XR/USD book and the American Perpetual stock perps sit in the same account; and the desk trades on top.

"If you have 100m tokens at 40, and they go to 400, you have 40b."— Saheb's rule of tokenomics, Aug 2023

Sources: World of Ohm (Aug 2022); Master / ManifestOS (Oct 2022); Saheb's rule of tokenomics (Aug 2023); Coin Denominated Reality (Nov 2023); Andromeda Capital Partners (Feb 2024); GLX and AP documents (2025–26)

The pods

A pod is a group of ideas with its own job, its own documents and its own next moves. The home page draws them around the core; every chapter below covers one or more of them.

Pod Job in the system Status (Oct 2026) Chapter
Rei (XR) The prime coin: supply, launch, ledger Designed; launch ladder proposed for 1 Nov 2027 IX
American Perpetual The regulated rail: perps on stocks first, then crypto, FX, metals, world indices Signing set final 30 Sep 2026; filings drafted, unfiled IV–VIII
GLX / Greyline The exchange build that became American Perpetual Superseded, kept as history and IP III
The desk Trading: the terminal, Hyperbull break list, funded-account trading Terminal built; rules from the funded-breakout thread still to import X
Origins The main-coin lineage, Manifest, the funds, stablecoins, Rifts Parked; feeds Rei's design II
Surfaces Where people meet the coin: Spaceyard, Nimbus in iMessage Designs and an app in progress IX, X
Capital and relationships MIAX, OCC, market makers, Silver Lake, Robinhood, Connamara Live relationships; see Appendix B III, VI, VII

How the pods depend on each other

The pods are not independent. Four links decide whether the whole system works.

  • The licence covers Rei unless it is carved out. The MPPLA pays American Perpetual 60% of MIAX's fees on every perpetual product on any underlying. A Rei perpetual on MIAX would be one of them, and the bitcoin and ether perps are both American Perpetual's planned second class and Rei's planned first products at MIAX. The Rei × MIAX brief says to carve Rei out of the deal and its IP assignment before either is signed, and before the 10% sale.
  • The IP assignment sweeps in earlier work. The September IP Assignment moves all pre-formation perpetual work into American Perpetual LLC. The June version kept five future ventures outside it (an FCM/broker-dealer, a trading app, white-label broker software, a DCM/DCO operating company and trading strategies). The desk and Rei both sit near that line.
  • One royalty, sold more than once on paper. Silver Lake's June package bought 3.5 of 36 royalty points; the September plan sells 10% of American Perpetual's units to one buyer after signing. Only one of these can be the live plan.
  • Relationships carry the launch. John Smollen (MIAX, EVP New Product Development) is described in the GLX documents as the warm-introduction layer to Citadel, SIG, Jane Street, DRW and Jump; Joe Ferraro (President, MIAX Products) is named on the signing path. The market-maker program, the crypto class and the Rei book all run through the same people.
The MPPLA gives American Perpetual 60% of MIAX's fees on any perpetual product, and Rei's plan puts an XR market on MIAX: decide whether Rei is inside or outside the licence before signature.

Sources: MPPLA v2.8 (30 Sep 2026); IP Assignment execution form (28 Sep 2026) and June IP Assignment; Silver Lake data room (Jun 2026); 10_GLX_Path_to_Close; Rei × MIAX brief (16 Oct 2026)

What this treatise is built from

The research read about 240 documents and artifacts: Saheb's Google Drive (the idea docs from 2021 on, the Greyline, GLX and Perps folders, the American Perpetual × MIAX data room in its 39-document "AI readable" edition, the closing set, the Silver Lake data room, the investor and counsel documents) and his Claude workspace (the Rei, XR, Trader in Time, Hyperbull, AP data room and other artifacts, plus the Claude Code sessions). Appendix C lists every source.

Three sources are not in yet, and the treatise says so wherever it matters:

  • iCloud Mail, including the threads with John Smollen. This environment cannot reach iCloud; the importers in the repository pull it into Drive once it is connected (see importers/).
  • claude.ai and ChatGPT chats. The Rei documents cite chats called "PRIME protocol" (25 May), "Prime token currency" (14 Jun) and "Private money system" (15 Jun), and there is a "Funded breakout coin leverage analysis" thread and a "zero-fee trenches" ChatGPT site. None of them are reachable from here; the harvest prompt and export importer bring them in.
  • iCloud Drive folders. Those that are not mirrored to Google Drive.

Where documents disagree (and they often do, because the plan changed fast), the treatise gives both values and flags the conflict in a Tension box.

How to read it

  • Chapter II is the 2021–2025 lineage: the coin, the fund and the first exchange sketches.
  • Chapter III is Greyline → GLX, the equity-perp exchange as it changed month by month.
  • Chapters IV–VIII are American Perpetual: the product and machine, the legal road, the deal, the street, and the expansion to crypto, FX, metals and world indices.
  • Chapter IX is Rei (XR), the prime coin, in full.
  • Chapter X is the desk: the terminal, the break list and the adjacent bets.
  • Chapter XI is coordination: the pods, their interfaces and the calendar that binds them.
  • Appendices A–C are the complete record: every dated event, every person, every source.

Search covers the whole text. Every section has a discussion thread in the Operating Room edition; in the shared edition, use the viewer's comments.

X → ∞ · Chapter I

Chapter IIOrigins — the coin, the fund and the first exchange (2021 → 2025)

Between March 2021 and October 2025 Saheb wrote dozens of crypto documents: plans, specs, term sheets, trade letters, spreadsheets and one-line notes. Read in order, they hold three ideas sketched again and again: a main coin that owns many product coins, an options-led fund that wants to become a prime bank, and a leveraged exchange where the house coin earns the fees. This chapter sorts every idea from the period into eight pods and ends with a dated timeline.

The short version

  1. The coin. In August 2022 one currency (OHM) prices everything in the "World of Ohm". By October 2022 it is a fixed-supply main token above a family of app tokens, paying dividends in USDC. In 2023 Saheb writes the rules down ("the ecosystem's coins must be less than the main coin") and, in Coin Denominated Reality, describes a public company whose main coin holds 100+ revenue-sharing product coins. This is the seed of the unified "Prime" coin.
  2. The fund. Andromeda runs from a $20m tokenized partnership through pooled ETH and SOL option trades to a $25m offshore fund brochure (3S Capital). Its stated end point: a market-making firm that grows "until we're the first legal crypto Prime bank in the world."
  3. The first exchange. The venue is sketched at least six times before GLX, from an OHM forex exchange with "Up to 500x leverage" (2022) and a BTCZPERP pair on the Z stablecoin (2023) to the "Andromeda Terminal" for multi-exchange perps (2024).
$1.3Mraised by Manifest "in 8 hours" (2021–2022)The only early project with outside money the documents confirm. It sunsetted.
100m × $400 = $40bSaheb's main-coin rule of thumb (Aug 2023)"If you have 100m tokens at 40, and they go to 400, you have 40b."

Four uses of "prime", none of them a ticker

No document from 2021–2025 names a token "Prime". The word has four roles:

Date Use What it is Source
2022-08-19 "fibonacci prime" Empire target of 99,194,853,094,755,497 E (Joules), the Fibonacci prime F(83) fibonacci prime
2022-10-04 "C-Prime" Smart-contract language for his own chain, "based off C#" Master
2023-04-01 "prime stairsteps" Supply schedule of the Z stablecoin; never defined Stablecoin
2024-02-13 "Prime bank" End state of the Andromeda market-making firm Saheb's 5 Year Plan

"PRIME perps" in the 2023 Altcoin sheet is Echelon Prime, a third-party gaming token. The "XR" note (2025-10-15) is a smart-glasses bundle with no coin.

Manifest: redeemable goods and the World of Ohm (2021 → 2023)

Manifest is the first project Saheb ran, the only one with confirmed outside money, and the brand he later put on his operating system. It began as a token-backed streetwear collective and ended as a plan to sell itself to Olympus DAO.

MNFST and the genesis hoodie (late 2021)

The FAQ (2021-12-19) calls Manifest "a decentralized streetwear collective, democratizing ownership of business". MNFST was a rebasing token in the OHM-fork style (a rebasing token raises every holder's balance on a schedule). NFTs sold for MNFST. Bonds took in WETH, sOHM and MNFST-OHM LP, so the protocol owned its liquidity: "we earn the swap fees because we own all our liquidity".

  • The genesis hoodie NFT sale (ERC-1155) was the token sale: "Every genesis hoodie NFT that was minted automatically staked 33 MNFST in the minter's wallet."
  • The treasury earned daily income on OHM and KLIMA.
  • Three verticals: Pipeline (a physical good paired with an on-chain proof of ownership), Streetwear (aimed at the "185 billion dollar streetwear industry") and Mafia (treasury growth through DeFi yield, partnerships and acquisitions).

The résumé (2025-11-12) dates Manifest October 2021 – July 2022: "Raised $1.3M and partnered with a factory in Los Angeles to produce redeemable NFT-linked products". The yami proposal (2024-01-11) says "raised 1.3m in 8 hours" and that the project "sunsetted".

The 2026 GLX investor document says "I built an algostable at seventeen, in forty days, and raised a million dollars at it in eight hours." The 8-hour raise matches Manifest, but in April 2021, before Manifest, Saheb called himself "a like minded 18 year old".

The pitch deck (April 2022)

The deck (manifest.gg, 2022-04-29) pitches "Digital Provenance of physical goods" in three lines: Manifest Bridge, open tooling that links a frontend to a Shopify inventory to mint redeemable NFTs; MaaS (Manifest-as-a-Service), "Design to Doorstep" merch for crypto communities through Los Angeles Fashion District factories, scalable to "a project every two weeks", redeemed at manifest.gg/inventory; and a luxury streetwear brand for "the crypto nouveau rich". Its tagline: "THEY WANT YOU TO OWN NOTHING, WE WANT YOU TO OWN EVERYTHING."

"until we actually launch our own token (not until there is regulatory clarity in the United States) *we learned our lesson launching a dao and token to early its not a good thing, it's a huge distraction that prevents focus on building."— Manifest Latest_Pitch_Deck, Apr 2022

The deck holds the first written trace of the exchange: "I've always wanted to trade options on miami condos, perpetuals on sneakers, japanese whiskey futures and long all these items and financialize these real world items." It names Manifold.xyz, RTFKT and Metafactory among competitors, and lists unlabeled names (RHINO, MILARK, THEHEYYMAN/GSV, R3LIC, ALTAMIRA, KRILL, DALA), probably collaborators.

The Olympus plan and the "World of Ohm" (August 2022)

On 2022-08-18 Saheb wrote: "Olympus acquires Manifest for 33m and me. Accept anything above 25m", and "I operator at Olympus for 25m". First he would buy out Milark's stake, pay out Dala and clear Manifest's debts. The plan's pieces, all priced in OHM:

  • Forex. "Olympus creates first decentralized forex trading exchange. Pairs OHM against currencies, synthetics. Purely frontend, not actually coins." OHM/USD, OHM/EUR and OHM/JPY, "Up to 500x leverage", positions denominated in OHM, lower fees for OHM holders, revenue to OHM holders. Lazer Devshop builds it, with referral links and a leaderboard.
  • Options on gOHM, BTC and ETH: "don't give out any APIs on how to market make because we'll install our own market making team" (the team is called "Gemini"). Revenue: fees, "being the casino", referrals and spreads.
  • Flow. Flow runs clothing drops in OHM through sudoswap-style pools ("stockx like markets") and pays quarterly stablecoin dividends; Olympus would own 16–17%. "CMill builds out Flow, with the 25m gnosis locked with me him and olympus officials, it's my personal piggybank."
  • A Cash-App-style super-app ("Cash App x Rainbow"): debit card (buy Mover), credit score (buy ArcX), borrowing, clothing, a TikTok-like creator app and the "Oddessy" marketplace, in five tabs.
  • Carbon: "bailout and buyout KLIMA, kill their token, keep the product allow people to carbon offset in OHM."

The outcome is not recorded.

Shared documents place Saheb in the OHM-fork scene: the PhantomDAO <> Olympus Proposal (2021-12-02; a Fantom fork run as a treasury-backed accelerator), the Concave Master Fork List (2021-12-05), the OhmieDay Drop FAQ (2022-03-05) and Mecasso (2021-11-10), NFT streetwear with swappable patches, a near-twin of Manifest Streetwear. He is named in none of them, and the deck's "launching a dao and token to early" does not say which DAO it means.

ManifestOS and C-Prime (October 2022)

Seven weeks later, on 2022-10-04, Master widens Manifest into an operating system:

  • Stack: "Streamable Operating System + Blockchain + Apps/Contracts written in C# Smart Contracts in C-Prime (performant blockchain specific language library based off C# similar to Nethereum.)" On mobile and PC, hosted on Azure.
  • Devices (year 3): "Devices sold as physically claimable NFTs for USDC on the ManifestOS only."
  • Apps with coins: curation, adtech bidding, a user-tag market, social, Wholesome OnlyFans, tax, cloud mining and storage, VR adult content, telecom and news; ten-plus games (Tac Nuke'm, forks of Candy Crush, Clash of Clans, Slither.io) and a PC shooter with wagers, skins and eSports betting "using any supported token, autoconvert"; Airbnb, Uber, Etsy, property, cars, StockX, Soundcloud, Craigslist and Upwork, each "for Crypto".
  • Finance: an "8-in-1 Bank SuperApp (Crypto Credit Score + Lending/Borrowing + Wallet Balance + Market (Stocks/Crypto) + Forex + Options + NFTs)" and a "Multiplayer Gambling SuperApp" with sports betting and prediction markets.
  • Organization: chain and OS in Seattle, apps and quants in Korea; "Me - (Alexander the Great) Get my marching orders from God" → Commanders → Troopers.

The last trace is "Manifest - liquidity pools on bitcoin" (2023-05-09): a title with an empty body.

Sources: Manifest FAQs (2021-12-19); Latest_Pitch_Deck.pdf (2022-04-29); Manifest plan (2022-08-18); Master, Organization (2022-10-04); Manifest - liquidity pools on bitcoin (2023-05-09); PhantomDAO <> Olympus Proposal (2021-12-02); Concave Master Fork List (2021-12-05); OhmieDay Drop FAQ (2022-03-05); Mecasso (2021-11-10); yami (2024-01-11); Saheb_Swaich_Resume.pdf (2025-11-12); GLX Investor Document (~May 2026)

The main coin and its product coins: the seed of Prime (2022 → 2025)

This is the most consistent design in the early work. For four years Saheb returns to one structure: a scarce main coin at the top, many cheaper product coins below it, revenue flowing up as USDC dividends, and one identity across every app. Each version adds a rule. Together they are the fullest description of a "Prime" coin written before 2026.

Version 1: one denomination (August 2022)

In the World of Ohm, OHM prices forex, options, clothing, NFTs, credit, the debit card and carbon; holding it cuts fees and earns exchange revenue. The coin is account, price list and dividend right at once.

Version 2: the Year-1 token stack (October 2022)

Year 1 turns the single currency into a family. Its first line is "Display All values in USDC/Our Token".

  • Tokens: "Main (adtech bidding) / Bank / Gambling / Social Media / User Tag".
  • The main token sells blockspace. Apps and advertisers "bid usdc for chain space/ads"; the chain splits the USDC "⅓ foundation, ⅓ paid to apps, ⅓ dividends to users, anyone can integrate the adtech to their app".
  • Product tokens: gambling ("if we raise at 300m, this token could be worth 10-30"); social ("could get to 100m (billions in years...US Tiktok is the goal)"); gaming ("super minor"); the bank gets "no token until markets, lending, credit scores".
  • Supply (linked Cloud Based Streamable Operating System note): "Connected Devices Are The Nodes / Fixed Supply / Stakers are eligible to dividends / 5G allows this to be possible now".
  • Currency becomes security: "All Tokens Pay Dividend eventually starts as currency -> security. Eventually Tokens can put out corporate bonds to raise debt and have financial sheets, this is basically trad companies on chain."
  • Governance: "Handle Token owners can vote on the fees to set on the protocol, foundation sets the default to 3%." "Dividend decided by foundation (decided by me, unilaterally @god)". "foundation holds all tokens, equity partners hold tokens, no vest."
  • Listing: "Get on coinbase/gemini stat."
  • A possible name: handles are priced in "USDC/REAL at current rates". REAL may be the intended main-token name; the note does not say.

Identity is the LIFE handle, bought in USDC at first OS start ("$3 plus change plus tax if kyc"; the change pays gas for 20–50 transactions):

Length Price Length Price
9 or more characters $3 4 characters $1,215
8 characters $9 3 characters $3,645
7 characters $27 2 characters $10,935
6 characters $81 1 character $32,805
5 characters $405 free tags (e.g. fire#4523) later

The ladder triples at each step except 6 → 5 characters (×5). Handles resell "in our token or usdc". Users choose a KYC path (credit card, custodial, taxed) or a non-KYC path (USDC on Solana or Ethereum). The plan budgets 100 staff at $200k, a $20m/yr burn.

The Blockchain note gives the philosophy: "ZK (privacy) & Low Storage (turn every device into a node)"; "Decentralization is foolhardy and can only be achieved through policy."; "Code isn't law. Law is Law."; "The tokens determinate the governance / Delegate tokens to foundation."

Version 3: the dividend airdrop ($SUP, February 2023)

$SUP - Support sells ChatGPT bots trained on a protocol's docs ($500 total; $400 for the web pop-up, $100 more for Discord; 16 SOL or $400 USDC). Its token model is one Saheb had "been wanting to do sometime":

"We buy tokens off the market and then we airdrop it to our users. I.e the people that purchased the chat bots"— $SUP - Support, 2023-02-03

The token: "Create a Solana token, using initial funds that we raised from the team and divvy it up amongst ourselves. List it on Openbook." The company buys its own token on the market and gives it to customers, so buybacks double as loyalty rewards. Phase 3 is an opt-in Solana search ("This is how we win the Solana Hackathon for tools/infra"), widened because "Solana is too small in of itself for this to be a 10m project".

Version 4: Saheb's rule of tokenomics (August–September 2023)

This page (2023-08-11, edited to 2023-09-10) is the closest thing to a constitution for the main coin. Its rules, verbatim:

  1. "If you create an ecosystem coin, the ecosystem's coins must be less than the main coin." A $40 coin should swap into 10,000+ ecosystem coins so "they feel like the 10000 can then become invaluable as the first coin."
  2. "Every product must have a token or face irrelevance." (Opensea vs Blur; Maestro vs Unibot.)
  3. "Sound tokenomics are actually valid, look at OX, RLB, UNIBOT". Soundness is "revenue generation any means necessary and revenue sharing any means necessary buy and burn airdrop yield, many many more."
  4. "21m supply is too little, nobody will buy the token if it's already 600" and "1b is too much. The main token needs to feel like a stock, stocks aren't priced at 9 zeros."
  5. Price in the tens of dollars: "too liquidy feeling under 10, but below a thousand dollars". "Faith that your token can go from the tens of dollars to tens of thousands ala early ethereum is the ideal sweet spot."
  6. "If you have 100m tokens at 40, and they go to 400, you have 40b." (META comparables imply 100x; Apple 1,000x.)
  7. "Liquidity is king, give tokens freely of the side projects but make sure the attention is directed to the most liquid proper project."
  8. "USERS UP = HOLD LET IT RIDE / USERS DOWN = SELL SELL SELL".
  9. "Have revenue, but don't be defined by revenue."

It adds that memecoins are destined "to have 1-2 large" (DOGE and SHIB; PEPE and Bitcoin), and "Shoutout sisyphus, hes a mercenary but aren't we all."

The same page says ecosystem treasuries can be drained "back to the main holders and the main team through decisive decisions like cooler loans, or borrowing without repaying". That conflicts with the regulated path Saheb later takes with GLX and American Perpetual.

Version 5: Coin Denominated Reality (November 2023)

Coin Denominated Reality (CDR), subtitled "Andromeda Fund Prospectus I" (2023-11-10, edited to 2025-06-25), opens: "Coin denominated reality means the way people, places, companies are denominated will be in coins." Its winner:

"a public company that holds a chain-main coin that holds in it's treasury ownership of various internet products coins which share revenue as dividends in usdc"— Coin Denominated Reality, 2023-11-10

The company pairs hardware and an OS that rivals iOS, Android, macOS and Windows with "hundred + revenue generating applications that have their ownership represented via coins." Verticals:

  • Proptech and social: Airbnb, Turo and Omegle "x crypto"; video and livestream shopping with "a way to bet on all of it".
  • Fintech: "Lend. Options. Perps."; "Crypto, stocks, and new corporations going public on main-chain coin"; multi-asset smart routing into stablecoins; prediction and idea markets; "bob's laundry service and frank's electrician company being bought and sold through one marketplace".
  • Gaming and media: tokens equal game revenue; "various merged coins can represent different ips"; mixed-reality games; pay-per-decision content (later Rifts).
  • Identity and software: a one-time $100 OS or device fee that funds the account and first username; AI software billed per millisecond; an AI-first OS with no browser.

CDR predicts the death of email as the internet account, of the browser and of "transaction based state model blockchains", and says "all L1s are the wrong direction". Its macro call: a bull market to Q2 2025, then a ~30% correction in Q3 2025. A minimized copy (2024-02-13) adds nothing.

Product coins elsewhere

What the lineage specifies for Prime

Supply Fixed; about 100m implied Cloud OS note; rule of tokenomics

Below it Cheaper product coins, 10,000+ per main coin rule of tokenomics

Supply never settles: "Fixed Supply" with no number (2022), about 100m (2023), 10m AND (2024), and "total of 250 tokens" for RIFT (2024, probably a typo for 250 Rifts).
The chain stance flips: own chain on Azure (2022), "all L1s are the wrong direction" (2023), then Rifts "on top of my existing blockchain" (2024). Nothing records a chain being built.
The early lineage pays holders from product revenue. The 2026 Rei/XR design keeps a prime-stepped supply but has "no fee, no burn" and "no peg, no redemption", so holders share new supply, not revenue. Which model Prime follows is open.

Sources: Manifest plan (2022-08-18); Year 1, Master, Blockchain, Cloud Based Streamable Operating System, Decenteralized Social Media (2022-10-04); $SUP - Support (2023-02-03); Sahebs rule of tokenomics (2023-08-11 → 09-10); Coin Denominated Reality (2023-11-10 → 2025-06-25; minimized 2024-02-13); yami (2024-01-11); Andromeda Term Sheet (2024-02-11); Proposal Rifts (2024-02-19); Brochure 3s capital.pdf (Oct 2024); notes 14 on the Rei/XR specification (Oct 2026)

Stable: Z, the prime stairsteps and the fiat rail (2023 → 2025)

Z is the only early token that uses the word "prime" in its design, and its stack names the first perpetual pair in Saheb's notes. It is backed by real estate, released in steps and defended by an in-house trading desk.

How Z works (April 2023)

The Stablecoin note (created 2023-04-01, 02:34–02:56 UTC) calls Z "My overcollateralized stablecoin":

  1. Building A is worth $60m. Borrow $10m against it.
  2. The building backs "10m Z printing in a prime manner".
  3. Lend the $10m to treasuries at 8%.
  4. Pay property-NFT holders their rent in Z; keep the real-currency rent (e.g. AED).
  5. "Z can be redeemed at market price for any currency at any time. CHF, AED, USD." That makes a Z forex market.
10m Zprinted against a $10m loan on a $60m building (Apr 2023)Loan proceeds earn 8% in treasuries; the peg is soft, "+-10% USD".

The peg is defended both ways:

  • Up: "Z up protection, unlocks stair steps in the prime to release greater supply, because greater demand." "Rate of stair step release increases based on demand."
  • Down: "Z down protection, rental income, arbitrage, trading is used to buy back Z to nominal ranges." A market-making team on Z/BTC defends "the soft peg of that 10% bid/ask spread".
  • Runway: "Stairstep runway: how many prime stairsteps of runway we have available." Near zero, take another real-estate loan, lend at 8% and print more steps.

In the example the property earns $6m a year; 7m Z is outstanding and 3m sits in prime steps (about six months of runway); a falling price is met by buying back Z with the $3m earned.

"When people are only dumping, it's fine because you have over collateralized so much, your interest yield is higher than what you're paying to defend the peg. It's escape velocity."— Stablecoin, 2023-04-01

The stack: Real Estate → Forex (Z/USD, Z/EUR) → Exchange (Z/BTC, BTCZPERP, other pairs) → Stable Coin (Z) → Portfolio Management (the loan book). The note opens with a separate idea: companies install dishes as gateways, users pay for internet in crypto, and "they get yield in Z."

What "prime stairsteps" means is never stated. It could be prime-sized tranches, unlocks at prime-numbered steps, or a Fibonacci-prime schedule like the 2022 fibonacci prime note. The 2026 Rei/XR specification gives an explicit version: total supply is always a prime, and each step moves to the next prime and mints the gap ("mint = next prime − this prime"). Whether that was the 2023 meaning is unconfirmed.

Z is a redeemable stablecoin backed by real-estate loans. The note does not discuss money-transmission or securities treatment.

StableStation (August 2025)

StableStation (2025-08-04) specs a Uniswap-style page that swaps USD and USDC both ways: "Fund your hyperliquid account in >2 minutes" (meaning under two). The flow: OAuth and Zero Hash KYC → Plaid bank link (an RTP-capable checking account; RTP is the instant US bank payment network) → a 60-second quote → deposit address or RTP/ACH instructions → webhooks → payout. "No Float Required: Zero Hash handles fiat custody and float / You never touch funds / You're just routing instructions + monitoring settlement".

The sample quote shows 100 USDC → $98.60 at "1 USDC = 0.986 USD" plus "Fee: $1.00". The rate alone gives $98.60, so the fee is inside it or counted twice; the spread is 1.4% either way.

A possible origin: "USDC App" and "USDC App Spec", owned by James Piazza III and shared with Saheb in July 2025, not read for this chapter.

Sources: Stablecoin (2023-04-01); StableStation (2025-08-04/05); fibonacci prime (2022-08-19); Drive search for "USDC App" (Jul 2025); notes 14 on the Rei/XR specification (Oct 2026)

Funds: from Andromeda to 3S Capital and the "Prime bank" (2023 → 2024)

Andromeda is Saheb's plan to fund everything else with option trades: make a name with a signature call-spread strategy, become a regulated options market maker, grow into a prime bank, then build the consumer-tech company. The asks grow; the documents confirm no outside capital.

The plan in two versions

5 year plan (2023-10-18) allocates a portfolio. A call spread buys one call and sells a higher one: cheaper than a single call, with capped upside.

Sleeve Weight Instruction or target
Main option leg 50% "Massively size into ETH call spreads via bit then kraken"; "a billiondollar play"
Altcoin options 20% "Use Bit to create options for FIL & others"
Snowball 10% Re-use unrealized profit as margin; "Snowballing 5m across 5 exchanges might go 500m"
Venture 10% NFT perps, NFT options, options teams
NFTs 5% Revenue NFTs (Aori Seats, RLB Rollbots)
Low-cap spot 5% "Longing 3m into altcoins might go 60m"

"Extra things are extra things, the main thing is the main thing."

Saheb's 5 Year Plan (2024-02-13) is the career version:

  1. Years 0–2.5: Option "Big Bet" Partnership LLC — airdrop farming, venture in options teams, and "a signature call spread roll strategy" that becomes "a story, a legend".
  2. Years 2.5–3.5: Andromeda Digital Asset Market Making Firm — a high-barrier US entity that "Market Makes for all crypto options", "risk-free delta neutral on my own money" (hedged against small price moves).
  3. Years 3.5–5: Paradise on Earth Tech Company — sideloaded app stores and an "OS level blockchain ecosystem" whose identity "is purchased akin to Windows for your PC", with "70-80 cryptocurrency tokens/applications".
  4. Years 5–10 — mixed-reality, crypto and AI devices; an OS "10% better than apple". Then he steps down.
"a legal financial base that I can expand on to later a Prime Bank if need be ... all the way until we're the first legal crypto Prime bank in the world"— Saheb's 5 Year Plan, 2024-02-13

The $20m partnership and the AND token

The Donnie Call prep (2023-12-27) sizes a "20m Fund / 2 Year Fund Size" and starts with Kraken's ECP/ISDA paperwork ("need to sign ECP with 10m in assets under management"). Four strategies: venture in options companies (PTF, OX, SDX.markets, LYRA); a low-leverage perp "snowball" on SOL and TIA; an ETH options ladder "1600 -> 2200, 2200 -> 3800, 3800 -> 7200 done over two years" plus SOL LEAPs (long-dated options); and airdrop farming through privately funded wallets: "It costs $400 to run for 100 wallets, running for 100 days is 40k for 100 wallets."

$20mAndromeda partnership target, via 10m non-transferable AND tokens (Feb 2024)Manager 30%, rising to 50% after a 3x return; no 2/20.

The Andromeda Term Sheet (2024-02-11): a Delaware partnership LLC on an Ethereum multisig; 10m AND tokens (implied $2 each); $5m maximum per investor; a 2–3 year term; votes in Telegram; $10m to Kraken Pro for US options, the rest to farming and venture; investors become multisig signers. No LP is named.

Single-trade pools followed, each into a Safe multisig with a Telegram group:

100xexpected return of three rolled SOL call spreads (Mar 2024)
The ETH letter pays the manager "20% of the Safe balance", not of profit; the SOL letter uses 20%/50% of returns. No letter records whether its trade was placed.

The SOL letter also names the Andromeda Terminal: hold SOL "via our Andromeda Terminal allowing us to build 9 figure perpetual positions using multiple exchanges from one access point." Its reasoning is the one that later drives GLX: "Using options is only viable when the leverage from them is cheaper than the perpetual leverage". No spec of the terminal exists.

What the books show (June and September 2024)

Two Fund Summary sheets record the actual position. Initial capital: 3.3 ETH ($10,335 in June, $7,888 in September). Liquid holdings: $8,913.16, then $23,400.17 ($12,257.17 on Hyperliquid).

  • Airdrop book: $21,919 sunk, estimated $162,500 low-end (7.4x) in June; $24,275 → $149,000 (6.14x) in September, across Rain.Fi, Lyra, Hook.xyz, Hyperliquid, Polymarket, Photon, Rainbow and others.
  • Sujiko arbitrage: $9,019.60 deposited, $61,637 PnL at fair value, 7 Sujiko NFTs; "a significant controlling stake of Sujiko is possible at launch".
  • Income ideas: a "Thalesmarket Mispriced Options Bot" (est. $3–10k a month), Lyra alpha farming (~$1,500 a week), Rainbow farming ("Could make 1m+"), PTF arbitrage, a KLIMA long.

ACM, outreach and 3S Capital (April → October 2024)

Outreach for Andromeda (2024-04-12) lists 42 LP targets, from funds such as Morgan Creek Digital and Multicoin to individuals such as CZ, the Winklevoss twins, Tim Draper and Michael Saylor. It is a target list only; a TOKEN2049 Dubai side-events list is dated the day before.

Operations for ACM (2024-06-16; most likely Andromeda Capital Management) runs "a next-generation crypto fund" on Telegram, AWS and X Premium, with livestream lifestyle content ("a la Nelk Boys") to recruit "young smart MIT quants" and win deal flow. "At our core, speed is essential." The fund exists "to build later on a crypto consumer tech company, which is the whole purpose of this fund."

Letter from the Founder (2024-10-06): "it's a traders market gone are the days of DCA spot and hold as long as you can", so "we use options on altcoins", because "I figured institutions would always be willing to sell options for yield". It closes with a generic fund template ($500k minimum, 2/20 over a high-water mark, 10% gate) and two other funds' terms.

$25m3S Capital raise target, $2m minimum ticket, launch "January 2025 expected" (Oct 2024)

The 3S Capital brochure (page 2 says "3S Crypto"; "3S" is never expanded) is the most finished fund document. It pairs "high-notional option contracts on select cryptocurrencies" (ETH and SOL first; counterparty Galaxy Digital) with an on-chain sleeve (UNIBOT, RLB, ORDI, points, bots) and "trade vaults" that make a farming strategy "one line-item on our return with one very simple PNL." Its thesis: "Option are the last bastion of edge due to how new they are in crypto", and "I would say we're at 1992, and by the end of next year we're going to be at the start of 1995". Projections to January 2026: BTC $127,000, ETH $8,000, SOL $430. Terms: offshore master-feeder (feeder funds investing through one master fund), 2% and 25%, quarterly redemptions, a 16-month gate; Horizons Law, Harneys, MG Stover, Copper.

The asks rise from $200k to a $25m fund while the September 2024 book holds about $23k liquid. No document confirms LP money arrived.

In the 2026 GLX investor document Saheb recalls this period: "At the time I was trying to start a crypto options fund". A Telegram friend running a quant book of "roughly 20 percent of Binance's daily volume" told him "2/20 is brutal" and offered a PM seat; he declined "because I wanted to launch something of my own".

Markets & tools: the derivatives conviction (2021 → 2026)

From 2021 on, Saheb's market views point one way: leverage belongs in derivatives, crypto options are underpriced, and perps are the default. His tools are small, but each sits on market structure.

The thesis

  • 2021. On 2021-08-04 Saheb copied Victor Bonhomme's tracker "2021 DeFi Next Big Thing: Decentralized Derivatives", which ranks venues by "Capital Efficiency" (volume ÷ TVL): Perp.fi 658.96%, dYdX 117.74%. Its live master shows most of that cohort collapsed by 2026.
  • 2023. The Altcoin sheet (2023-10-25 → 2024-01-06) sets the rules: "Perps - if it has perps, use perps"; "Options are cheap leverage because the entire crypto options market is not priced in yet so get leverage through options wherever possible."; "The baseline is a 10-20x via SOL spot". Tier 1-A includes BDP, TAO ("we should build a perp position"), LYRA ("Crowning the winner of defi options"), DETO and PTF (CEX options) and PROPY (tokenized real estate). Crypto Winning (2023-10-05) puts "Options Meta", "NFTFi Meta" and "Stablecoin Meta" in Tier 1.
  • 2024. Crypto Options Protocol 0->1 3 Months (2024-01-31), after Rick Rubin: "I have seen every single option protocol known to man in the crypto industry and believe I can do so much better. I can do it so much faster too." No design follows.

The tools

  • Hook NFT Options Bidding bot (2023-10-28): "Capital efficient chest (airdrop) farming of the Hook.xyz option protocol by placing IV bids using a bot." It bids implied volatility (IV, the volatility an option price implies) minus 10 on out-of-the-money strikes within 20% at the farthest expiry, re-bids when IV moves 10 points, and cancels all on any fill. V2: "Use black scholes price using a limit order and auto update the limit order." The June 2024 book: Hook points "as if I had invested 100s of ETH with only $25".
  • Thales mispriced-options bot (by June 2024), estimated at $3–10k a month.
  • Sujiko (2023-10 → 2024): in the Solana Hyperdrive hackathon sheet, "a derivatives exchange for NFT perpetual futures" across four chains "with as little as $1 of collateral". Saheb held seven top-rarity Sujiko NFTs and ran a Sujiko arbitrage; his formal role is not stated. It is his first hands-on perps venue.
  • Airdrop farming: 10 Backpack wallets across Sujiko, Sanctum, Clone and Solayer (2024-01-31 → 06-13); Zeneca's Blast list, the Blast Gold table, Rainbow's RGB points FAQ and a 2021 airdrop list show the habit from 2021.
  • Launch sniping: the Banana Spreadsheet (2024-02-28 → 04-03) logs Banana Gun token-launch snipes.
  • Bitcoin Ordinals playbook (2023-02-11): hold grails ("Sell 80-120 btc"); flip 3k+ PFPs from 0.11–0.3 BTC to 1–2 BTC. Clive's Ordinals list and a Bitcoin Bears OTC book date from the same week.
  • Trade stubs: "The Greatest Solana Option trade done by man." (2024-06-11): "'21 Mid Cycle Fractal results in 20% weekly candle." A sheet titled "Copy of May 2024 Altcoin Shorts" sits in the same folder, unread.
  • Skins price list (2023-03-13): 25,145 CS:GO, Dota 2 and Rust withdraw prices ($2,144,002.72 total), probably for the Master skins market.

Research Saheb received

In February 2024 Justin Drake (Ethereum Foundation) shared his based-preconfirmation working-group directory (about 393 people) and his "fixing fragmentation" notes: Ethereum L1 as the shared sequencer that orders rollup transactions, with preconfirmations (collateral-backed promises of inclusion), slashing for safety and liveness faults, and about 10% of proposers needed to opt in. Fragmentation is costed with Metcalfe's law: Arbitrum's $10B of $20B rollup TVL is a 4x loss; 1/50th of Ethereum's $500B is 2,500x. Saheb is not in the directory.

Other research on the shelf: MEV.fyi's paper index (2023-09-28), an OtterSec audit of Flooring Lab (2023-10-21), the Hyperdrive sheet and dddappp deck (2023-10-22; a possible judge role, unconfirmed), 1kx's DePIN role map (2024-02-22), Allbridge's February 2024 report ($123,196,911 volume), a "Sonder" smart-money trading guide (2023-01-20), a DFinance ICP one-pager, NFT P&L and CryptoPunks rarity sheets (2021–2022), and an irrelevant Rust jobs board.

The bridge into GLX (March 2026)

Untitled0.ipynb (2026-03-24) pulls three years of weekly US stock data (157 dates) from Polygon ("Massive"), ranks tickers by average daily notional, keeps those active at least 30% of the time, flags sfp_eligible at an average close of $5 or more (SFP: security futures product, the legal class GLX used), and writes glx_notional_rank.csv. The run returned no rows. It is the first listing screen for the equity-perp exchange.

Security: the notebook's first cell contains a plaintext Polygon/Massive API key. Rotate it. The key is not reproduced in this treatise.

Sources: 2021 DeFi Next Big Thing: Decentralized Derivatives (2021-08-04) and live master; Crypto Winning (2023-10-05); Altcoin (2023-10-25 → 2024-01-06); Hook NFT Options Bidding bot (2023-10-28); Crypto Options Protocol 0->1 3 Months (2024-01-31); Airdrop Farming Solana (2024-01-31); Banana Spreadsheet (2024-02-28); BTC NFT One Pager, Clive's Ordinals Project List (2023-02-11); Bitcoin Bears Listings (2023-02-12); prices (2023-03-13); The Greatest Solana Option trade (2024-06-11); Hyperdrive Projects (2023-10-22); Fund Summary June/Sep (2024); sequencing & preconfs (shared 2024-02-09); fixing fragmentation (shared 2024-02-24); shared research as dated; Untitled0.ipynb (2026-03-24)

Media & XR: Rifts, the multiplayer web and the glasses (2022 → 2025)

The consumer layer is one account that pays per use everywhere: films, games, social and markets. Rifts is its most developed product, and RIFT the clearest product coin.

Rifts and RIFT (February 2024)

Proposal Rifts - The Netflix of the future. (2024-02-19, edited 2025-02-07) proposes VR films with spatial audio and photorealistic Unreal Engine 5 "interactive pauses": look down a sniper scope, peek through a window, take a Bandersnatch-style choice, switch character.

  • No subscription. Each decision is a micro-payment from the "OS-level blockchain account", about $25 per 90-minute Rift; replays pay again.
  • RIFT. Investors "instantly receive dividends every time a decision inside of a Rift occurs"; an 8% owner earns $2 per $25 viewing.
  • Raise. "$2b raise at a 25 billion dollar FDV" (fully diluted valuation) for 250 Rifts and a production company, built "on top of my existing blockchain, and hardware company".
  • Pitch framework appended: Story → What → Why now/you/this → How → FINANCES → Wrap it up.
"pay-as-you-use will be all the rage as enabled by instant payment settlements using cryptocurrency rails."— Proposal Rifts, 2024-02-19

One identity, many surfaces

  • Multiplayer web (2024-04-06), in full: "Users have their OS identity (crypto account / handle) and traverse the cryptoeconomy together eventually expanding to experiences such as Rifts, content consumption, games, markets."
  • XR (2025-10-15), in full: "Smart glasses + Controllers bundle / Gamemodes: Zombies, Pokemon, MMO / Android XR". No token. ("XR" returns in October 2026 as the ticker of the Rei coin, covered later.)
  • Mixed reality elsewhere: CDR's real-world zombie game with 3D-printed weapons, the 5 Year Plan's MR devices, and "Vision Pro Coins/NFTs" in Crypto Winning's Tier 1.
  • Wholesome OnlyFans (2022-08): 20–30 minute video sessions for a $20–30 flat fee in SOL, on an Internet Computer back end, funded by Psychedelic; later "Omegle, but specialized" (2022) and "Omegle x crypto" (2023).
  • Decentralized social media (2022-10): "Customer pays entrepreneur pays influencer promotes to customer"; "Customers can own social tokens of influencers, and bet on their success."

Sources: Proposal Rifts (2024-02-19, edited 2025-02-07); Untitled document "Multiplayer web" (2024-04-06); XR (2025-10-15); Coin Denominated Reality (2023-11-10); Saheb's 5 Year Plan (2024-02-13); Crypto Winning (2023-10-05); Manifest plan (2022-08-18); Master, Decenteralized Social Media (2022-10-04); notes 14 on Rei/XR (Oct 2026)

Real assets & the energy standard (2021 → 2023)

Beside the coins sits a physical ambition: own land, buildings and vehicles, rent them in crypto, and measure the empire in energy.

fibonacci prime and the energy unit E (August 2022)

Written the night after the Olympus plan (2022-08-19), fibonacci prime scores an "empire" in Joules (E) across money, people and land. Its constants: "12.09 cents / 1000 = 0.0001209$ = 1 Joule"; "8271 Joules = $1"; "1370000 joules per square meter" of land; "78 million Btu per person = human capital"; "$23,386 per acre"; and "1 LINK = 1 $ / 1$ = $1 / 1 UNIT OF ENERGY = $1".

Gates's land plus Tesla's value and workforce comes to "so far only abt 20quadrillion E". The path to the target: 1 million acres, a $5 trillion company, 500,000 workers, and "count each citizen in your empire"; scope is to be revisited "During mid 50s".

99,194,853,094,755,497 Ethe empire target, the Fibonacci prime F(83) (Aug 2022)"THIS IS WHAT I CHOOSE". F(47) = 2,971,215,073, also a Fibonacci prime, appears too.
"Alexander did better than me, fine. He's great. But I can do more in this reality for technological advancement because it's faster, and more people chant my name so more Energy."— fibonacci prime, 2022-08-19

No token is designed, but "1 UNIT OF ENERGY = $1" reads like a peg: E is the earliest candidate for an energy-standard unit of account, and the earliest use of primes as milestones.

The real-estate crypto empire (March 2023)

How to build a real estate crypto empire in the world. (2023-03-29) studies Homebase, "Solana's first security token offering": one SPV (special-purpose vehicle) per home, NFTs as SPV shares, monthly rent, under Reg D 506(b) (unlimited accredited investors, up to 35 sophisticated non-accredited, no public marketing). Saheb's design: "Single company, single NFTs, optionality to fractionalize after the core purchaser has owned the NFT." Three days later, Z pays those holders' rent.

"80 companies" (September–October 2023)

An untitled deck: "With my initial option trading earnings I will create 80 companies". The first three are AirBnB, Turo (supercars first) and private-jet charter "but crypto", owning the assets; the charter pays "usdc dividends to holders, pay usd to company bank account". Strategy: "Snowball digital-physical businesses with superior distribution, global liquidity, and global awareness starting from the higher end to the lower-consumer end."

"Automated-order-routing swapping multi-assets directly into stablecoins, crypto, bankaccounts, dividend payments at the speed of the internet. (!! recurring theme)"— Untitled presentation, Sep–Oct 2023

The Prosperity Game (2021)

The earliest document in the set (2021-03-28) doubles $1,000 a day for 19 days ("Spent: $524,287,000"), mixing DeFi blue chips (ETH, MKR, SNX, UNI, COMP; a "Miami Blockchain/Ethereum Business") with Austin and Miami rentals. That pairing of crypto and property never leaves the later work.

The first exchange, sketch by sketch

Every early venue shares three traits that survive into GLX and American Perpetual: leverage through perps or synthetics, fees that flow to holders of the house asset, and the house's own market-making.

Two shifts happen. The underlying moves from synthetic FX and crypto to US equities (CDR in 2023, the screen in 2026). The legal stance moves from a non-KYC path and "you want to avoid taxes and be our guests" (2022) to Kraken's institutional rails (2024), then the regulated MIAX path of GLX and American Perpetual (later chapters).

Open questions and risks

In order of how much they matter for the unified design:

  1. "Prime stairsteps" is undefined. Ask Saheb whether the 2023 meaning matches the 2026 prime-gap schedule.
  2. The main coin has no fixed supply number: "Fixed Supply" (2022), about 100m (2023), 10m AND (2024).
  3. Revenue or new supply? Early coins pay holders from product revenue; the 2026 design shares new supply with "no fee".
  4. The chain: own chain (2022), anti-L1 (2023), "my existing blockchain" (2024); none recorded as built.
  5. Legal exposure in early language: "decided by me, unilaterally @god"; "borrowing without repaying"; "you want to avoid taxes and be our guests"; a non-KYC path; 500x forex; dividend tokens that become securities; a redeemable real-estate stablecoin; tokenized LP interests with investors as multisig signers.
  6. Unrecorded outcomes: the Olympus deal, the $20m raise, the ETH and SOL trades, 3S's launch, the yami grant, the Rifts raise, the StableStation build.
  7. Forecasts to check: a ~30% drop in Q3 2025 (CDR); TAO's August 2025 halving as "the peak of the bull" (Altcoin); ETH to 7,200 (Donnie call); SOL $480 in 2024; BTC $127,000 by January 2026 (3S).
  8. Security and privacy: rotate the notebook API key. The sources also hold wallet addresses, a multisig address and a phone number; none are reproduced here.
  9. Unread titles in the same Drive folder: Copy of May 2024 Altcoin Shorts; Future Greyline Ideas; Equity Perp Proposal.docx; Swaich — The Starbase Shuttle; Stock Option EV; AI Untapped as of Feb17th; Copy of Partners; Proper Usage of Social Media for a tech billionaire.; 2028; The "Impossible" Task; How to get to what I really want; 1Real Frontend App.

X → ∞ · Chapter II

Chapter IIIGreyline → GLX — building the equity-perp exchange (Nov 2025 → Jun 2026)

Between 23 November 2025 and early June 2026, one product — a perpetual future on a single US stock — passed through six business models. The contract settled by March 2026. What kept changing was the part Saheb would own: a retail app, a full licensed stack, equity in a MIAX joint venture, a revenue share as introducing broker, the whole MIAX futures complex, and finally a royalty on the fee pool. This chapter walks the models in order, then covers what they shared.

A perpetual future ("perp") never expires. It is held to the stock by the funding rate — the hourly payment between longs and shorts that ties the perp to the stock.

The arc in one table

Each model kept the product and moved the economics. The pattern: Saheb designed a layer, found MIAX already held it or that owning it added little to the fee pool, and moved one layer up.

Dates Model What Saheb would own Why it gave way (per the documents)

26 Nov–2 Dec 2025 Own DCM + DCO (clearinghouse) + FCM (futures broker), $100M seed All three licences — "four companies" Licences cost "$100M+ and 3-5 years"; MIAX already held all three
5 Dec 2025–9 Jan 2026 MIAX joint venture (NewCo) under an MOU ≥40% or ≥80% of NewCo Replaced on 27 Jan by an alliance; no reason recorded

Basket perps and the $100B pathway (Nov 2025)

The idea began as a wedge product Saheb did not expect to sell. Its job was to raise $20–40M and open a door at MIAX.

The first plan (23 Nov) had four steps: index perps on MIAX; a CFTC no-action pilot for single stocks; an own DCM built during the pilot; joint SEC/CFTC rulemaking. The legal trick: a single stock or an index of fewer than 10 names is a security-based swap, but a broad index of 10+ names with ≤30% in each is CFTC-only. So the app would trade thematic baskets — "AI", "Datacenters", "SMRs", "Oil & Gas", "Drones" — as "Trade Ideas on leverage", up to 20x, adjusted daily because "technology doesn't support hourly funding rates". Design: "Liquid Glass design under 2025 ios design standards + an orange theme". Todo: Persona for KYC, RQD or Apex for clearing, "MM: Find one to invest, Jump."

"Obviously, I don't actually expect these products to be very popular, it's mainly just to have a horse in the race"— Executive Summary, 2025-11-23

"The pathway to a 100b exchange" (25 Nov) added a second bridge, the Continuous Single Stock Future: a security future with a 10-year expiry and a daily cash adjustment as funding, capped at 15% margin (6.6x). "This is how coinbase did their 5 year BTC/ETH derivative." MIAX's rulebook already had security futures: "the shell exists".

On 26 Nov Saheb wrote to Bridget Harris at Founders Fund. His thesis was the "anatomy of a trade". A crypto exchange is a DCM, DCO and FCM in one. The US splits them and settles in daily batches, so a US perp venue needs all three. "We're basically building four companies at the same time." He asked for $30M and set aside on-chain perps: "The real money would be in the legal US single stock perpetuals on a DCM we control."

"The difference between a 1b brokerage app, and a 100b+ financial empire."— Founder's Fund letter, 2025-11-26

The first Greyline documents (27 Nov – 2 Dec) asked for a $100M Series Seed to license an own DCO, FCM and DCM. The take rate went from 10 bps to a 4–6 bps all-in stack: DCM 1.5–2.5, DCO 0.3–0.5, FCM 0.5–1.0, IB 0.5–1.5 and funding spread 0.5–1.0. The downside was a licence-resale floor of $150–200M, citing Kraken–Small Exchange ($100M) and Polymarket–QCEX ($112M) — "a risk free option on the future of markets". The TAM took US spot at ~$20T/yr and 5 bps: 10% capture → $1B revenue; 68% → $6.8B.

Why it gave way. The licences were the slow, costly part — "$100M+ and 3-5 years to obtain independently" — and MIAX held all three. On 5 December the plan flipped from build to partner.

Same-day valuations disagree: $100M pre-money (Greyline_Pitch_Deck_Full) vs $300M pre / $400M post (Greyline.docx, modified a day later).

Sources: Executive Summary, Todo list, Swaich, The pathway to a 100b exchange (2025-11-23 to 25); Founder's Fund (2025-11-26); Untitled "GREYLINE" (2025-11-27); Greyline.docx and Greyline_Pitch_Deck_Full (2025-12-01/02); Greyline_OnePager (2025-12-11)

The MIAX joint venture (Dec 2025)

In December Saheb stopped trying to own the licences and asked MIAX to contribute them. Greyline would bring product, regulatory strategy, engineering and customers.

The proposal went through three versions in five days:

  • v1 (5 Dec) — the first contract spec: AAPL, MSFT, NVDA, TSLA, AMZN, GOOGL, META; 1 share; 24/7/365; 5% initial (20x) / 2.5% maintenance margin; funding every 4 hours, capped at ±0.50%; an insurance fund, then auto-deleveraging (ADL — force-closing winning positions to cover a loss).
  • v2 (6 Dec) — "three exemptions that matter": CFTC 39.14 settlement, 39.13(g)(2) margin period (1 day → 4 hours), and SEC security-futures margin. "Exemption #3 is the entire SEC conversation." A 2.5 bps fee split DCM 35% / DCO 35% / FCM 30%. 30% of ~$500B/day of US equity volume → $13B+/yr.
  • v3 (10 Dec) — maker/taker 1/5 bps all-in; 50 bps liquidation fee; a worked NVDA liquidation; a Designated Market Maker rebate of +0.5 bps; $12B+ revenue at scale.

The MOU (11 Dec, 22 Dec, file state 9 Jan) set up a NewCo. MIAX would contribute its DCM, DCO and FCM licences and the Onyx matching engine, under 12 months' binding exclusivity. It would name an SVP-level liaison and co-invest in the seed once Greyline had raised half. The 22 Dec draft added a work-product clause: if MIAX launched equity perps within 24 months without the JV, it would compensate Greyline.

"Ownership in the railroad, not a ticket to ride it."— Greyline_OnePager, 2025-12-11

The 20 Dec data room added three documents. A Partnership Overview promised "Zero development risk" for MIAX and a 2027 launch. A regulatory memo used the Bitnomial precedent: "MIAX files, Greyline builds". A technical appendix classified the product as a Security Futures Product (SFP) — the joint SEC/CFTC category created in 2000. "We will file as a true perpetual—no artificial expiration. If regulators require the nominal term, we add it." The same day a spreadsheet, GLX_Revenue_Model, used "GLX" for the first time. On 29 Dec the Technical Architecture brought in Dorman Trading as FCM.

Why it gave way. The documents do not say. On 27 January the Heads of Terms replaced NewCo equity with an exclusive alliance and a fee split.

JV equity floor: Greyline "no less than forty percent" (22 Dec draft) vs "no less than 80%" (unsigned file modified 9 Jan); which went to MIAX is unknown.
The OnePager lists an "MOU with MIAX" under "What we've secured"; every MOU copy in Drive is unsigned.
Funding interval: every 4 hours (5–10 Dec proposals) vs "hourly settlement" (20 Dec Partnership Overview).
Funding cap: ±0.50% per interval (5–10 Dec) vs ±0.10% (29 Dec architecture) vs no cap (Mar 2026 onward).
Funding interest term: SOFR − borrow (v1) vs SOFR + borrow spread (v2, final Equity Perp Proposal) vs none (v3, and rejected in the Mar spec).
The SFP margin floor is stated as 15% (most docs), "statutory 20%" (Feb deck), 15–20% (Nov memos) and "100% margin" under Reg T (Dec proposals).
The Tech Appendix's method gives TSLA 12–15% initial margin (7–8x), yet its proposed parameters give TSLA 5% (20x).
The Technical Architecture is headed "January 2025" but was created 29 Dec 2025 — almost certainly a typo for 2026.

Sources: Greyline_MIAX_Equity_Perps v1/v2/v3 (2025-12-05/06/10); Equity Perp Proposal (2025-12-06); Greyline_MIAX_MOU unsigned (2025-12-11, mod 2026-01-09); MOU_Revised_Draft (2025-12-22); MIAX_Partnership_Overview, Regulatory_Pathway_Memo, Regulatory_Pathway_Technical_Appendix, GLX_Revenue_Model.xlsx (2025-12-20); greyline_technical_architecture (2025-12-29)

Strategic Alliance, Greyline Markets and the GLX name (Jan–Feb 2026)

January turned the joint venture into an exclusive, open-ended revenue-share alliance. Greyline became the introducing broker: the customer-facing layer that sends orders to MIAX and clears through Dorman.

  • 11 Jan — engineer brief: "We're the IB—the product layer." The diagram reads MIAX ↕ FIX/FEI ↕ "GLX Platform" ↕ Dorman. Funding is hourly.
  • 17 Jan — Entity Requirements, a ~370-task checklist: $50M at $400M pre, 6 months to launch, $20B/day target. Sign Citadel Securities as "Primary liquidity provider", then Jump; "Sign 2 BLPs".
  • 21 Jan — Strategic LOI for market makers: "Founding Liquidity Partner" status, MFN fee tiers, and a non-binding seed indication of $2.5–5M, $5–10M or $10M+.
  • 27 Jan — Heads of Terms and long-form Strategic Alliance Agreement between Greyline Markets, Inc. and Miami International Holdings, including MIAX Futures Exchange (DCM + DCO) and Dorman Trading (FCM).

The alliance terms (both drafts unsigned):

  • "Perpetual Futures" means funding-rate contracts "across all asset classes including equities, equity indices, digital assets, commodities, rates, and foreign exchange."
  • Mutual exclusivity; indefinite term; exit only by consent or an uncured breach after 90 days.
  • Maker/taker fees 80% Greyline / 20% MIAX; liquidation fees 100% Greyline; no DCO or FCM fees; funding earns nobody anything.
  • Greyline funds the insurance fund at Dorman and pays for the CFTC exemption. Either side may walk if there is no relief in 24 months.
  • Greyline owns the IP and MIAX gets an exclusive licence. The deal survives a MIAX change of control.

The February investor deck (saheb@greyline.markets) showed the result. Trading 24/5 (Sunday 6 PM – Friday 5 PM ET); hourly funding; VeriClear as DCO; the Backstop Liquidity Provider (BLP) as "Our Innovation"; Connamara Systems as technology partner; live trading in Q3 2026. Fees 2/5 bps, 2.8 bps net to Greyline; $10B/day → ~$1B revenue. "Only firms committing liquidity get access to invest in the round." End state: "THE UNIFIED DELTA-ONE VENUE — Perps → spot → options. Full broker-dealer."

The name. "GLX" moved from a spreadsheet (20 Dec) to the platform (11 Jan) to the brand: by March–April documents carry glx.com, and Path to Close budgets to buy the domain and the @GLX handle. No document says what GLX stands for.

The Feb deck says "MIAX signed" and "Exclusive alliance signed"; the HoT and SAA in Drive are unsigned, MIAX's signature block blank.
DCO identity: "MIAX DCO / MIAX Clearing" (Dec) vs "VeriClear" (Feb onward).
Trading hours: 24/7/365 (Nov–Dec 2025) → 24/5 (Feb–Mar 2026) → "23/5" (Apr 2026 onward).
Valuation drift: $400M pre (Jan) → "valuation set by lead" (Feb) → no-cap SAFEs (Mar–Apr) → $10B pre (May).

Sources: greyline-engineer-brief (2026-01-11); Greyline Entity Requirements (2026-01-17); Strategic LOI Template (2026-01-21); MIAX_Heads_of_Terms and MIAX_Strategic_Alliance_Agreement (2026-01-27); Old Investor Deck ("February 2026"); MIAX.pdf (May 2026)

The introducing-broker build (Mar–Apr 2026)

By March, Greyline was a Guaranteed Introducing Broker under CFTC Rule 1.57 in a four-entity stack. It ran "the brain" and was paid 90% of the fees on its own customers' trades plus a 20% royalty on everyone else's.

Entity Registration Role
MIAX Futures (Onyx) DCM Matching, mark-price and funding engines, fee collection, surveillance
VeriClear DCO Novation, clearing-member margin, daily Fedwire, default waterfall
Dorman Trading FCM Segregated customer funds, deposits, statements, tax forms
Greyline Markets Guaranteed IB UI, shadow ledger, margin engine, liquidation controller, funding calculator, API

Greyline never touches customer money. Orders go to Onyx by FIX; fills come back on a drop copy. By 3:10 PM ET Greyline sends Dorman a file of funding, fees and liquidation charges, and VeriClear settles each clearing member's net by Fedwire at ~3:30 PM. Three books must agree: Greyline's sub-account funding, Dorman's customer funding and VeriClear's charge. Dorman executes Greyline-triggered liquidations without re-checking them.

The economics were "dual-lane":

  • Lane 1: 90% Greyline / 10% MIAX on Greyline-originated volume.
  • Lane 2: a 20% "Product Origination Share" off the top of every other trade. A $1M Robinhood taker trade at 5 bps ($500) pays Greyline $100, Robinhood $200 and MIAX $200. A Citadel market-maker trade at 2 bps ($200) pays $40 and $160.
  • Liquidation fees go 100% to Greyline. Dorman keeps 50% of deposit interest; MIAX keeps market-data revenue.

The March Executive Summary asked MIAX for "$12M SAFE + signed Strategic Alliance Agreement" and put MIAX's 10% at ~$130M in Year 1. The Partnership Deck sold the BLP to market makers as "Robinhood PFOF economics applied to liquidations" and the auction as low-risk: "MIAX has built PRIME four times. This is the fifth port." It quoted MIAX CEO Tom Gallagher: "We built the church for Easter Sunday." In April the BLP Memo turned this into a coalition: sign Founding BLP MOUs before a mid-2026 "Round 1" meeting with regulators. "The Founding cohort exists once."

Why it gave way. Saheb later called this "Plan A" — IB, teach MIAX's stack, royalty, own frontend — and "then the ambition kept growing". Path to Close also warned the HoT "will not become the instrument MIAX signs": MIAX papers such deals through MIAX Products, LLC, as with its 2024 Bloomberg index licence.

Revenue split: 80/20 (Jan HoT/SAA, Feb deck) vs 90/10 + 20% royalty (Mar spec, later HoT/SAA); Path to Close's table shows 20/40 and 40/60 while calling the numbers "stable".
Deposit interest: "Dorman retains 100%" (Deck slide 9) vs 50% (slide 10, Executive Summary, SAA).
MIAX projections: Year 2 $25B/day → ~$320M and scale $50B → ~$640M (Exec Summary) vs Year 2 $50B → ~$640M and scale $100B → ~$1.2B (Deck).

Sources: 05_GLX_Complete_Technical_Specifications (V1, Mar 2026); 08_GLX_Architecture_Reference (v2.5, Apr 2026); 01_Executive_Summary and 02_MIAX_Partnership_Deck (Mar 2026); 03_MIAX_Heads_of_Terms; 04_MIAX_Strategic_Alliance_Agreement; 01_Greyline BLP Memo (Apr 2026); 10_GLX_Path_to_Close (Apr 2026); GLX_Investor_Document (~May 2026)

The contract, hours, mark and index

Every later model shares one contract: a cash-settled, never-expiring future on one US share, traded 23 hours a day, five days a week. "No greeks. No expiry. No roll."

  • Size: 1 contract = 1 share (~$200 notional); $0.01 tick; USD collateral at the FCM.
  • Universe: 7 megacaps (Dec 2025) → top 30 incl. SPY and QQQ (Feb) → top 100+ (Mar–Apr), scaling to 2,000+. A 24 Mar 2026 notebook ranked US tickers by daily notional and flagged SFP eligibility at an average close ≥ $5.
  • Hours: Sunday 6 PM – Friday 5 PM ET, maintenance 5:05–5:40 PM; overnight priced off ATS venues (Blue Ocean, MOON, Bruce).
  • Mark price (used for margin and liquidation) = median(Last Trade, Best Bid, Best Offer), the FTX method, computed inside Onyx. Halted or at Sunday open: Mark = Index + Last Premium.
  • Index = ICE Consolidated Feed NBBO midpoint.
  • Controls: collar ±10%; premium band ±5%; circuit breaker ±10% → 2-minute halt.
  • Corporate actions: no dividend adjustment (to avoid §871(m)); splits automated off DTCC; spin-offs, M&A and special dividends above 10% go reduce-only and cash-settle.
  • Position limit: 2.5M shares per account (the §41.25 default of 25,000 × 100-share contracts); market makers exempt.
Stock splits are handled "at IB level" (Tech Spec §26) vs at DCM/DCO level (§9/§11); the Playbook says standardise on §9/§11.

Sources: 05_GLX_Complete_Technical_Specifications (Mar 2026); 01_Executive_Summary (Mar 2026); Old Investor Deck (Feb 2026); Untitled0.ipynb notional rank (2026-03-24)

Funding

By March 2026 funding was a pure pass-through of the perp's premium over the stock, averaged over an hour and paid hourly. It never changed again.

The formula, verbatim:

Funding Rate = TWAP( (Mark − Index) / Index ) / 24

  • The premium is sampled every second (3,600 samples) and locked at each :00: drain give-ups, snapshot positions, lock. No recompute, no bust.
  • Payment = Position Notional × Funding Rate, long to short or back. The venue earns nothing.
  • No cap, no damper, no interest term (interest without a dividend term would bias shorts). Paying 23 times a day at /24 realises 95.8% of the premium daily.
  • Example: AAPL index $200, perp averaging $200.40 → 0.20% premium → 0.00833% an hour; a 10,000-share long ($2M) pays $166.67.
  • Settlement: novated by the DCO, with variation-margin character, itemised as a "Funding Adjustment" line in the one daily Fedwire.
  • Precedents: Coinbase Derivatives / Nodal Clear Filing #2025-33 (the /24) and FTX. Contrasts: Bitnomial (8-hour, ±0.05% clamp + 0.01% interest), Cboe PBT (±0.002 clamp), Coinbase (EMA α = 0.75).
"Funding is not a fee and not a settlement — it is the economic substitute for expiry."— GLX_Funding_Mechanism, ≥ Jun 2026

That sentence carries the legal case: if funding substitutes for expiry, the contract is still one "for future delivery" and fits the SFP framework.

Date Interval Formula Cap Interest
5 Dec 2025 4 h Premium + (SOFR − stock borrow) ±0.50% Yes
6 Dec 2025 4 h Premium + (SOFR + borrow spread) ±0.50% Yes
10 Dec 2025 4 h Mark below index → shorts pay longs ±0.50% No
29 Dec 2025 4 h clamp((Perp TWAP − Index TWAP) / Index TWAP, −0.1%, +0.1%) ±0.1% No
Mar 2026 → 1 h, 23/day TWAP((Mark − Index) / Index) / 24 None No
≥ Jun 2026 1 h, per ICE-covered hour Same None No

Because dividends flow through funding, two side effects follow: a §871(m) withholding problem for non-US holders, and — the Investor Document notes — a predictable ex-dividend funding trade.

Funding count: fixed 23 per day (Reg Pathway, whitepaper) vs "rate count = ICE-covered hours" including overnight (Funding Mechanism).
The Op Doc calls Bitnomial's funding-as-variation-margin "on-point" (§31) but requires the "Coinbase Rule 1131 archetype, not the Bitnomial" (App. G.4).

Sources: greyline_technical_architecture (2025-12-29); Greyline_MIAX_Equity_Perps v1–v3 (Dec 2025); 05_GLX_Complete_Technical_Specifications §4 (Mar 2026); 06_GLX_Regulatory_Pathway (Apr 2026); GLX_Funding_Mechanism (≥ Jun 2026); GLX_Complete_Operational_Document (May 2026); GLX_Investor_Document

Margin: the square-root curve

Margin scales with the square root of position size. Small positions get up to 25x; very large ones carry more margin than the 15% statutory floor the petition asks to remove.

The basis is FTX's practice and market-impact research: Kyle (1985), Almgren–Chriss (2000), Bouchaud/CFM, and a 2026 study of 942 Tokyo stocks showing impact grows with √size. Initial margin fraction (IMF) is what you post to open; maintenance fraction (MMF) is where liquidation starts.

Version IMF MMF k (mega/large/mid/small) Leverage cap
Dec 2025 proposals 5% flat 2.5% flat — 20x
Dec 2025 Tech Appendix 99th-pct daily move + buffer 50–60% of IM — ~20–25x SPX, 7–8x TSLA
Mar 2026 spec (older) max(Base_IMF, k × √shares) max(2%, 0.6 × k × √shares) 0.0001/0.0002/0.0003/0.0006 25/25/20/10x
Apr 2026 Reg Pathway max(IMF_floor, k × √Q) max(MMF_floor, 0.5 × k × √Q) 0.0001/0.0002/0.0003/0.0006 25/25/20/10x
May 2026 spec, whitepaper max(Base_IMF, k × √shares) max(2%, 0.5 × k × √shares) 0.0001/0.0003/0.0005/0.0006 25/25/20/10x
Clearing briefing, Op Doc max(1/selected_leverage, k × √Q) max(0.5%, 0.5 × k × √Q) 0.0001/0.0002/0.0003/0.0006 None at venue; FCM leverage dial

NVDA at $135 and k = 0.0001, the same in every version:

  • Below ~160,000 shares ($21.6M) the 4% floor binds: 25x.
  • At 2.5M shares ($337.5M, the limit): 15.81% IMF, 7.91% MMF, 6.3x.
  • At 20M shares: 44.72% IMF, $1.21B of margin, 2.2x — against ~$405M under a flat 15%.

"√Q runs MORE conservative than flat 15% across the institutional range; the floor binds only at small retail size."

In April the slope was tied to each broker's insurance fund (Exhibit N): a bigger fund at the clearing FCM earns a gentler curve.

Tier Insurance fund k floor NVDA shares where it bites
1 $50M 0.0003 ~17,778
2 $250M 0.0002 ~40,000
3 $500M 0.00015 ~71,111
4 $1B+ 0.0001 ~160,000

On 2.5M NVDA shares a Tier 4 Goldman posts $53.4M; a Tier 1 broker posts $160.1M. Accounts run normal above IMF, reduce-only between MMF and IMF, and liquidate below MMF in ~600 ms rounds. Unrealised profit counts as margin — "the single biggest UX difference between offshore venues and a traditional futures broker".

Large/mid k: 0.0002/0.0003 (older spec, Reg Pathway, Op Doc) vs 0.0003/0.0005 (latest spec, whitepaper); HOOD is mid-cap at 20x in one, large-cap at 25x in the other.
MMF floor 2% (specs) vs 0.5% (Clearing briefing, Op Doc); small-cap 6% (Reg Pathway) vs 2% (whitepaper); multiplier 0.6 vs 0.5.
The Op Doc forbids publishing Base_IMF or a leverage cap, yet its clearing-member deliverable lists "Base_IMF, max leverage cap" and still cites Exhibit N.
The Op Doc's "100× equivalent at the methodology floor" does not follow from k = 0.0001 with no Base_IMF.
