Liquidation: the BLP auction, the insurance fund and "no ADL"

Saheb's most original design was a liquidation auction for market makers that would rule out offshore-style cascades and auto-deleveraging. It was the centrepiece from January to April 2026. In May he dropped it.

How it grew. December's waterfall was crypto-style: trader → insurance fund → ADL → DCO guaranty fund, with Min Insurance Fund = Max OI × Stress Gap × Liquidation Rate × Slippage. On 5 January came the BLP idea. In February BLPs absorbed positions at the bankruptcy price. By March the auction came first and ADL was gone.

"Backstop Liquidity Providers between the insurance fund and ADL mechanism."— Future Greyline Ideas, 2026-01-05

The mechanism. When an account breaches maintenance, its broker cancels open orders and sends the minimum partial close to Onyx as a LIQUIDATION order. Onyx broadcasts a request to every registered BLP. BLPs bid sealed within 500 ms, must take the full size, and cannot bid below Mark × (1 − 5%) for a long or Mark × (1 + 5%) for a short. Best price wins; the trade prints off-book at zero fees. If nobody bids, the fallback is IOC orders in 25% tranches, 200 ms apart. It is a simplified port of MIAX PRIME (Rule 515A), MIAX's options price-improvement auction.

BLPs must bid every request. In return they get exclusive auction flow and 0/2 bps book fees. Target cohort: Citadel Securities, SIG, Jane Street, DRW, Jump. The flywheel: deeper book → higher limits → more open interest → more auction flow → deeper book.

Why ADL cannot happen. VeriClear sees only each clearing member's net position, and force-closing one FCM's customer to cover another's loss would breach CEA §4d segregation. The comparison is 10 Oct 2025 offshore: $19.1B liquidated in 24 hours, and Hyperliquid's ADL closing $2.1B across ~19,000 wallets in 12 minutes.

"No cascades. No ADL. The risk engine doesn't allow them."— 01_Greyline BLP Memo, Apr 2026

The insurance fund moved four times: a segregated DCO account (Dec) → a Greyline house account at Dorman fed by the 50 bps liquidation fee, seed "$XXXM (open item)" (Jan–Mar) → mandatory per-broker funds gating leverage (Apr) → a "$1B+ first-loss layer" (Connamara.pdf) or a $1B fund from a Series B "sized to rival Binance's" (Durban memo).

Why it gave way. "And then I dropped it. Not deferred — dropped." V1 liquidates on the Onyx order book, "the permanent architecture", because "the BLP architecture was solving a problem that does not exist on this venue… MIAX is already the attraction." The Op Doc removes auction and fund and accepts recourse: "This is not a crypto-style never-owe world."

BLP status: centrepiece of the whitepaper, Series A deck, Reg Pathway and Clearing briefing vs "dropped… permanent architecture" (Investor Doc), which still mentions "Liquidation auction fills (under V2)".
MIAX.pdf and Connamara.pdf (May) still route all liquidations through the auction in the month the Op Doc deletes it.
Insurance fund: mandatory IB-level fund (Reg Pathway), $1B Series B fund (Durban memo), "$1B+" (whitepaper) vs "The venue operates no insurance fund" (Clearing briefing).
"No GLX-IB customer ever owes money on a liquidation" (Arch Ref) vs recourse, "not a crypto-style never-owe world" (Op Doc).
The Clearing briefing says "Customer accounts never go negative" (Part 11) and that a negative residual "is a debit… pursued through ordinary collection" (Part 5).
Without the margin grant "the BLP auction program does not function" (Reg Pathway) vs it "would function mechanically at any margin level" (Clearing briefing).
ADL placement: the 5 Jan idea puts BLPs between the insurance fund and ADL; by March the auction is first and ADL is "structurally impossible".

Sources: greyline_technical_architecture (2025-12-29); Future Greyline Ideas (2026-01-05); Old Investor Deck (Feb 2026); 05_GLX_Complete_Technical_Specifications §7, §14 (Mar 2026); 01_Greyline BLP Memo and 02_Greyline_Founding_BLP_MOU (Apr 2026); 06_GLX_Regulatory_Pathway; GLX_Clearing_Margin_Briefing; GLX_Memo_Durban; Connamara.pdf; GLX_Investor_Document; GLX_Complete_Operational_Document §33

Fees and the fee pool

Revenue is one line: a flat maker/taker fee in basis points on notional. Everyone — MIAX, clearing members, brokers and Saheb — is paid from that pool.

Participant Maker Taker
Retail / customer 2 bps 5 bps
Registered market maker / BLP 0 bps 2 bps
BLP auction fills 0 0
Liquidation fee (to insurance fund) 50 bps of notional —

"Fee = Contracts × Price × BPS rate." No volume tiers. The blend: "~40% of trades × 5 bps + ~50% × 2 bps + ~10% × 4 bps = 3.4 bps blended per trade", assuming market makers trade ~85% maker and retail ~80% taker. Other documents use 2.5 bps (Series A deck) or 3.5 bps (revenue model, Feb deck).

When Split Source
Dec 2025 DCM 35% / DCO 35% / FCM 30% MIAX proposal v2
Jan 2026 80% Greyline / 20% MIAX; liquidation fees 100% Greyline HoT, SAA
Mar–Apr 2026 90/10 on own flow + 20% royalty on other flow Tech Spec, Arch Ref
May 2026, operator ~100% retained Year 1, ~79% mature; brokers get 40% of customer fees Whitepaper
May 2026, licensor GLX ~30–36% / integrators ~35–45% / MIAX remainder Fee Architecture
May 2026, licensor Integrators 40% / Saheb 36% / MIAX 24% Investor Document
Sept 2026 60% of transaction-fee revenue + $40M at signing AP compilation

The 40/36/24 split gives integrators 40%, then divides the remaining 60% 60/40 between IP holder and operator: 0.6 × 0.6 = 36%. Benchmarks: the "25–33% rule" floor; S&P DJI/CME ~15–19%; S&P DJI/Cboe ~30–50%; MSCI ~25–35%; Hyperliquid HIP-3's 50% as ceiling.

Fee rail: daily in the VeriClear Fedwire (latest Tech Spec, Playbook, Clearing briefing) vs a monthly invoice (older spec, Arch Ref, Fee Architecture, Op Doc: "The DCO sweeps fees with variation margin" is false).
The Fee Architecture calls the prior "~79% retention" and "20% origination royalty" "history, not terms".

Sources: Greyline_MIAX_Equity_Perps v2/v3 (Dec 2025); MIAX_Heads_of_Terms (2026-01-27); 05_GLX_Complete_Technical_Specifications §2, §15; 08_GLX_Architecture_Reference; glx_whitepaper §03; GLX_Fee_Architecture_Reference; GLX_Investor_Document §8; AP_ALL_DOCUMENTS_ONE_FILE (2026-09-30)

Market size: $463T × 2.2 ≈ $1Q

The May 2026 method builds the market bottom-up from what customers would move onto perps, then multiplies by market-maker turnover. It lands near one quadrillion dollars a year of cleared notional.

The napkin. Crypto perps trade 3–5× spot. Applied to $277T of annual US cash-equity trading, that gives $830T–$1.4Q. The conservative 2× gives $554T.

The bottom-up. Ten V1 categories that fit the SFP wrapper sum to $425–501T of customer demand, mid $463T. Three carry ~93%: equity-index ETF perps $260T, single-stock perps $110–140T, and migration from total-return swaps $35–55T. Multiply by a 2.2× market-maker circulation multiplier (range 2.0–2.5×, from CME E-mini, Cboe SPX, Eurodollar and crypto dealer shares): ~$1Q, 3.6× spot. About 75% of the ~$1.2Q equity-index complex stays put (mandates, non-linear products, §1256 tax, plumbing); the absorbed quarter is mostly retail 0DTE flow.

"Perp is ODTE without the expiry."— Advanced_TAM_Methodology, May 2026

Phase 2 adds foreign index, FX, foreign single stocks, commodities, rates, foreign TRS and direct crypto: $515–885T × 2.2 ≈ $1.5Q more, ~$2.5Q by 2035.

To money. $1Q × 3.4 bps ≈ $340B/yr; 36% ≈ $122B. On the $554T floor: $188B pool, $68B royalty — against CME's $6.5B of 2025 revenue.

TAM bases drift: ~$20T/yr spot (Nov 2025) vs ~$500B/day (Dec) vs "$100T+" (Feb) vs $277T/yr (Apr onward); $275T vs $277T between decks.
Category fee pool: ~$150B (Competitive Memo) vs ~$125B (Connamara docs) vs ">$100B" (MIAX.pdf) vs ~$340B (whitepaper, Investor Doc).
Perps' share of crypto trading: 75% (Series A deck) vs 84% (Investor Doc).
The documents flag their own $1Q and $11.8T figures as aggressive; the sanity check rests on the crypto perp-to-spot ratio.

Sources: Advanced_TAM_Methodology (May 2026); GLX_Royalty_Economics (~2026); glx_whitepaper §01–03; GLX_Investor_Document §7; 09_GLX_Competitive_Memo (Apr 2026); GLX_Series_A (May 2026); Greyline.docx (Dec 2025)

Regulatory asks and the fee exemption

The product lives inside an existing statute: the Security Futures Product framework of 2000, idle since OneChicago closed in 2020. Two substantive asks unlock it; the rest adapt rules to a 1-share contract.

  1. Margin floor. Replace the 15% minimum (Exchange Act §7(c)(2)(B)(iii); SEC Rule 242.403) with the √Q method, via an SEC §36 order paired with CFTC Rule 41.42(d).
  2. Future delivery. Confirm that hourly funding satisfies "contract of sale for future delivery" (CEA §1a(44); Exchange Act §3(a)(55)(A)), via a CFTC §4(c)(1)(B) class order plus an SEC interpretation.

Supporting items: staff guidance on credit extension (Release 8107) and risk disclosure (§41.41(b)); and a large-trader threshold rescaled from 1,000 contracts (only 1,000 shares) to ~100,000 shares.

The fee exemption. Regulatory fees were sized for OneChicago's 100-share contracts. At 1 share and $1Q of notional (5T round-turns a year) they would swamp the trading fee. At the standard NFA rate ($0.02 a side per contract) a 1,000-share trade pays $20 a side, more than the exchange fee on stocks under ~$40.

Fee Today Asked
SEC §31(d) $0.0042 per round-turn → ~$21B/yr Full §31(f) exemption (2002 precedent, 16 days)
NFA Bylaw 1301 $0.04/RT → ~$200B/yr Restore the 2012 rate, $0.00008/RT
FINRA TAF $0.00011 + $0.014 minimum $0.00008/RT, minimum waived
Total $221B/yr (22.10 bps) futures account; $25.6B (2.56 bps) securities account $400M/yr (0.04 bps)
"The 2012 parity framework lapsed during a four-year volume gap. GLX is the volume returning."— Fee Exemption v7, May 2026

Channel and timing. The plan rides joint SEC/CFTC harmonisation: the Atkins–Pham statement (5 Sept 2025), Michael Selig sworn in as sole CFTC Commissioner (22 Dec 2025), and the Atkins–Selig MOU (11 Mar 2026), with staff contacts Teply (SEC) and Tente (CFTC). Round 1 is a joint meeting with CFTC DMO, CFTC DCR and SEC Trading & Markets, MIAX present, Founding BLP MOUs signed. Petition at weeks 12–15, orders at months 5–8, first trades 7–11 months after the alliance closes. The 30 May 2026 CFTC policy statement (with BTCPERP) routed equity perps to Rule 40.3 review plus joint SEC review, and the one-pager reframed the petition as class authority: "reviewed once, self-certified forever".

May documents add launch blockers: MIAX Futures' §6(g) registration (6–12 months); VeriClear's §17A registration and a $2B capital test; ISG admission (Bitnomial took ~5.5 years); a perpetual addendum to the risk-disclosure statement. The documents name their own risks: who holds office after 2026, the §7(c)(2)(B)(iii) reading ("the only genuine legal question"), and the calibrated weekend-gap loss.

Exemption count: two (Competitive Memo) vs "four exemptions mapped" (Exec Summary) vs six (Playbook, Reg Pathway) vs 4 + 3 + 3 (Op Doc) vs three gating asks (MIAX.pdf).
SEC §31: a reduced $0.00008/RT rate (Playbook, MIAX.pdf) vs full exemption (Fee Exemption v7).
CFTC route: Rule 40.3 prior approval (Tech Spec §3, Op Doc) vs "§40.6 self-cert" (Tech Spec §10, Feb deck) vs 40.5 (Exec Summary).
March 2026 harmonisation credited to "Atkins and Pham" (Connamara.pdf) vs Atkins and Selig (Op Doc).

Sources: 06_GLX_Regulatory_Pathway (Apr 2026); MIAX_Execution_Playbook (v2); Fee Exemption.pdf (v7, May 2026); GLX_Regulatory_One_Pager_UPDATED (≥ Jun 2026); GLX_Complete_Operational_Document (May 2026); GLX_Investor_Document §6; GLX Complete Technical Spec §28

Tax

The contract is a §1234B security future, not a §1256 contract: no 60/40 treatment, and three practical problems.

  • Reporting. Every disposition gets its own 1099-B line, so a million round-turns can mean a million lines. Wash-sale, straddle and constructive-sale rules apply; the UI warns on long stock + short perp (§1259).
  • §871(m). Non-US holders face 30% dividend-equivalent withholding. With dividends priced through funding, the proposed base is funding over an ex-date window — which needs an IRS private letter ruling (PLR).
  • Funding payments. Character unsettled; ordinary income on a 1099-MISC is the conservative default. A PLR costs ~$38,000 and takes 6–9+ months.
Holding period: "short-term… regardless of holding period" (Apr Reg Pathway) vs the long side follows the holding period (May tax memo).
§871(m): a launch blocker (Op Doc §35, tax memo, one-pager) vs "Not applicable in V1" (Op Doc §37, Tech Spec) vs "doesn't apply in V1 wrapper" (TAM doc).

Sources: GLX_Tax_Characterization (v1, May 2026); 06_GLX_Regulatory_Pathway App. B; GLX_Complete_Operational_Document §35, §37; Greyline Entity Requirements (2026-01-17)

Tech stack and architecture

The build was split so MIAX added a few engines to systems it already ran, Dorman did almost nothing new, and Greyline (with Connamara) built the broker-side brain. The asset was the shadow ledger: a live per-customer position book that is the only gate for pre-trade margin checks.

January sketch. ~60–80K lines of code: ledger 8–12K, risk 5–8K, liquidation 4–6K, API 8–10K, web 15–20K, mobile 12–18K. Go back end (QuickFIX/Go), PostgreSQL + TimescaleDB, React, React Native, AWS. "The platform runs itself—humans handle exceptions."

March split. MIAX: mark and funding engines, a feed extension carrying mark/funding/index, the PRIME auction port, a bps fee engine from MIAX Pearl Equities. VeriClear: a "Funding Adjustment" settlement line. Dorman: "intentionally minimal" — accept a daily file. Greyline/Connamara: ledger, sub-accounts (20), margin engine, liquidation controller, funding calculator, reconciliation, ops dashboard, API, web and native apps.

"The shadow ledger is the rulebook expressed as backend computation. The unified frontend is the rulebook expressed as customer experience."— GLX_Tech_Stack_Memo, ~Apr 2026

The ledger is "service, not library": one deployment per broker, same code, isolated data. As Greyline Solutions it became a 12-component package for any broker: ledger, themable frontend ("Robinhood looks like Robinhood"), API, connectivity, FCM file generator, KYC, payments, certification and more. "The rulebook is the asset; the infrastructure that runs the rulebook is the moat."

Layer Technology Owner
Onyx extensions, VeriClear config C++ / MCS MIAX — Zach
Shadow ledger OPEN: C++ on Connamara EP3 vs Java 21 + LMAX Disruptor "Decision belongs to Dan"
Connectivity, public API Go (QuickFIX/Go) Connamara
Frontend, terminal, mobile TypeScript/React, TradingView, React Native Brett Harrison
Ops and data Python, Kafka (audit log), Postgres, ClickHouse; AWS with Direct Connect to MIAX NY5 Connamara

API: REST + WebSocket, no withdraw scope, and an "OpenAPI 3.1 spec at /v1/openapi.json" that "any AI agent can ingest". Payments: Plaid bank transfer, wire, and USDC via Circle Mint + CCTP across seven chains, converted into USD at Dorman's BMO Harris segregated account — the only crypto rail in the GLX corpus, an on-ramp rather than collateral. The Op Doc later locks "USD-Fedwire-only collateral".

Design. Design Spec V1.2 (12 Mar 2026) rated Hyperliquid (top tier), Lighter (minimalist), Bybit (standard) and Binance (comprehensive) as good; dYdX (dull), OKX (complex), QFEX (legacy) and Ostium (unrefined) as poor. The palette moved from November's orange to a "Lunar" grey/blue: "we want to be the venue for 100m+ notional positions."

USDC custody: "At no point does USDC sit in a Greyline-controlled wallet" vs a build item for "Greyline-controlled wallet infrastructure on the CCTP mint-native chain".
USDC and mobile are P0 in the latest spec but V2 "Low" and "Medium" in its own roadmap.
The Build Manifest says "no premium band, no funding cap, no FVL"; the Op Doc keeps the ±5% premium band and makes Fair Value Limits a launch blocker.

The MIAX relationship: Skunk Works, the SAFE and the Path to Close (Mar–Apr 2026)

MIAX was the only US group with a DCM, a leverage-capable DCO and an FCM under one roof, so every model ran through it. By April the relationship had a 50-day plan with named people on both sides.

MIAX in the documents. NYSE-listed since 14 Aug 2025; four options exchanges, 18.2% share; 2025 revenue $431M. Its clearinghouse, from the Minneapolis Grain Exchange, was grandfathered on 21 Dec 2000. On 21 Jan 2026 it sold 90% of MIAXdx (ex-LedgerX) to a Robinhood/SIG venture.

Skunk Works (Mar). MIAX's development calendar was full "through Q1 2027". Greyline proposed a 2–4 person unit across MIAX, VeriClear and Dorman, on MIAX payroll, with 0.25–0.50% Greyline equity, milestone-vested; its "Director of Special Projects" would join the GLX board.

The MIAX SAFE. $12M from MIH, post-money YC, 15% discount, no cap ("MIAX converts at 85%"). Path to Close added Performance Warrants: $0.01 strike, 3.5% of fully diluted post-Series A, four milestone tranches, 10-year expiry, plus a permanent MIH board seat.

Nine instruments MIAX would actually sign: Product Licensing Agreement (MIAX Products, LLC); Exchange Services Framework + Rulebook Side Letter; VeriClear Clearing Side Letter; Guaranteed IB Agreement (Dorman); Onyx connectivity; Exchange Data Agreement; Insurance Fund Segregation Letter; the $12M SAFE; the Performance Warrant.

The pairings.

  • BLP outreach: Saheb + John Smollen, MIAX EVP New Product Development — 30 years at Goldman Sachs and Cboe, Cboe Member Vice Chairman in 2006. "Smollen opens doors and runs the MOU conversation with Saheb."
  • The definitive stack: Saheb + Joe Ferraro (Joseph W. Ferraro III), SVP & Deputy GC of MIH and President of MIAX Products since 10 Nov 2025 — "one person at MIAX who can drive and sign the full stack".
  • The Series A: Smollen → SIG → Dooley → Egon Durban at Silver Lake.

The 50 days. Days 1–14: entity, SAFE and warrants signed, $12M wired, glx.com bought, soft calls to Citadel and SIG, then DRW and Jump. Days 14–35: data room, Zach Dexter as COO, CCO hired, MOUs signed, Head of Markets, 2–4 Founding BLP SAFEs. Days 35–50: Durban, diligence, term sheet. Named risks: SIG is "triple-hatted" (path to Silver Lake, MIAXdx co-owner, BLP target), Dexter's non-compete, warrant tax at MIH. The April Regulatory Pathway lists the alliance as the "Current bottleneck", dependent on "Joe Ferraro / Tom Gallagher".

The May Series A deck shows "MIAX STRATEGIC ALLIANCE [SIGNED]" and "$12M SAFE SIGNED"; Mar–Apr documents present both as asks, and every alliance draft in Drive is unsigned.
The Connamara memo cites "Four CFTC-registered entities under a signed strategic alliance"; the HoT/SAA in the data room are unsigned.
MIAX market cap: ~$3B (Exec Summary, Deck) vs ~$5B (Investor Document).

Buy the stack (May 2026)

Pure licensor, 36%, and the bridge to American Perpetual (May–Jun 2026)

The last GLX model owns nothing operational. GLX licenses the product to MIAX and takes 36% of the gross fee pool, forever.

"GLX's sole role is product originator."— GLX_Complete_Operational_Document, May 2026

Under this "On Margin" framing GLX is not a broker, holds no customer money, and is "not in the money path". There is no auction, insurance fund or ADL; each FCM liquidates through ordinary orders on the lit book. Every broker builds its own shadow ledger. "Connamara is a vendor doc, not a counterparty."

The royalty. "36% of the gross fee pool on every perpetual-futures product MIAX lists under the rulebook, in perpetuity, regardless of underlying reference, methodology, or marketing name." Saheb's points sit in GLX LLC, a Delaware single-purpose entity whose only asset is the MIAX royalty agreement, against "the binding term sheet I have with MIAX". Capitalised at 15–20×: ~$1.0–1.4T on the $68B floor, ~$1.8–2.5T on the $122B topline.

The offer. "I am offering you up to 3.5 of my 36 points, at $30 million per half-point" — $210M for 9.72%, implying $2.16B for the whole. Proceeds to Saheb personally, free and clear; exclusive to Silver Lake Long-Term Capital and Egon Durban. Reasons: fast capital, a decades-long partner, and the next things — a consumer cross-margin app and ventures in "space, minerals, and energy".

Why licensing. Every operating layer is "a teaching exercise" that only "participates in the operating margin around the fee pool I already have a share of." "If I am the only person in America who knows how to do any of this, why would I teach anyone but MIAX?" And: "That work didn't fit me. The licensor seat did."

The bridge. "On May 20, 2026, MIAX Futures and OCC signed a clearing and settlement agreement expressly to cross-margin MIAX Futures products." The Op Doc leaves open "Whether GLX clears at OCC instead of at VeriClear", which would remove the §17A and capital-test blockers. By 30 Sept 2026 the AP data room shows the answer: American Perpetual LLC (Originator), OCC clearing, "60% of the products' transaction-fee revenue" plus "$40,000,000 wired at signing", Connamara as backup build team. The AP compilation never names GLX or Greyline; the lineage rests on the same founder, venue, spec and pathway.

Royalty sale: 3–5 points for $100–350M, implying $0.72–4.2B (Royalty Economics) vs ≤3.5 points at $30M per half-point, implying $2.16B (Investor Document).
Headline basis: ~$68B/yr with $122B "not used" (Royalty Economics) vs $122B topline and $68B "sub-conservative floor" (Investor Document).
Royalty drift: 20% (Mar–Apr) → ~30–36% band → 36% (May) → 60% (AP), with the base moving from "gross fee pool" to "transaction-fee revenue".
What MIAX actually agreed is unsettled: a "signed" alliance (deck), a "binding term sheet" (Investor Doc), or the stale acquisition (MIAX.pdf).

The competitive memo

The argument: a regulated category goes to the first venue that lists it and stays there for decades. GLX's edge was meant to be the pathway and the risk engine, not the app.

"The first exchange to list captures the entire category for fifty years."— 09_GLX_Competitive_Memo (v54), Apr 2026
  • Precedents: CME equity index futures (1982), CME Treasuries (1977), ICE energy (early 2000s).
  • Category: $86T of CEX perps in 2025; 2× $277T US cash trading → $500T notional → "~$150B/yr" fee economics, about Visa plus Mastercard. GLX target: "tens of billions annually".
  • Offshore proof: Hyperliquid — $2.95T cumulative, $844M revenue, eleven people, no VC. Trade.xyz — >$100B cumulative in five months, $600B annualised, and an official S&P 500 licence (18 Mar 2026). 10 Oct 2025 — $19.1B of cascading liquidations.
  • Moat: relief attaches to MIAX's rulebook, so a rival needs its own 12–24 month petition. Kraken's $550M Bitnomial deal lacks an options business and an auction. "Hyperliquid runs on legal contingency. GLX runs on durable statute."
  • Rival to ally: the Feb deck listed Architect ($35M, Bermuda) as competition; by April its founder, Brett Harrison, was running GLX's frontend.

24/7 via Discovery Bounds. V1's 23×5 is "the one structural non-parity item versus offshore". The fix is trade.xyz's Discovery Bounds: while the stock is closed, the mark is held to a band around the last external price, with a ratchet: upper_trigger = reference_price × (1 + discovery_bound × oracle_threshold), lower symmetric. Liquidation is suspended outside the band. Tech Spec bands: mega ±5% with one reset (±15.8% effective), large ±7% (±22.5%), mid ±7% hard, small ±10% hard. Target: 6–12 months after V1.

Discovery Bounds: ±5/7/7/10% with 1/1/0/0 resets (Tech Spec) vs ±1/2/3/6% with 3/3/3/2 re-anchors (Op Doc).
Hyperliquid: $844M revenue (Competitive Memo, whitepaper), $857M (Investor Doc), ~$900M profit and $4T volume (deck, Durban, Connamara memo); 12 employees (Nov 2025) vs 11.

X → ∞ · Chapter III

Chapter IVAmerican Perpetual — the product and the machine

American Perpetual is a perpetual future on one U.S. stock. It has no expiry date and settles in cash on 100 shares. An hourly funding rate holds it to its stock. It is listed on MIAX Futures' Onyx platform, cleared at OCC (the Options Clearing Corporation), and regulated jointly by the SEC and CFTC as a security futures product (SFP), under the framework as written. The design is finished on paper: a 124-page Unified Technical Specification, a 38-page OCC Clearing Brief, two playbooks, 73 pages of runbooks and a pre-written notice for every event. As of the data-room compilation of September 30, 2026, nothing was filed and nothing was signed.

Only one part of the product is new. Funding, the hourly payment between longs and shorts that replaces expiry, is cleared by OCC as ordinary variation margin, inside the daily settlement OCC already runs. Everything else is restarted rather than invented.

"This product is the OneChicago OCX.NoDivRisk contract with the expiry removed and clearing-grade hourly funding added."— MIAX Brief End-to-End, Sept 2026

This chapter walks through the contract and the machine around it. The legal path is Chapter V. The licence economics (a 60% royalty on Covered Fees and a $40,000,000 signing payment) are Chapter VI and appear here only where the machine touches them.

The contract on one page

The contract is a standard security future with one change: it has no expiry. Every term is fixed once for the whole class, in Rulebook Chapter 43 and Part A of the Specifications Supplement. After that, each name is one row in a schedule.

Term Value Where it lives
Type Cash-settled, no-expiry single-name equity SFP; joint SEC/CFTC jurisdiction Rules 43.1, 43.7
Unit 100 shares; multiplier ×100, "never adjusted for any corporate action" Rule 43.4
Tick $0.01 per share = $1.00 per contract; USD Rule 43.5
Expiry None. No contract months, no roll; final settlement only on a Terminal Settlement Event Rules 43.7, 43.16
Universe One class; 101 inaugural names "SPY through MIAX"; later names by day-before certification Rules 43.2, 43.22
Symbol Ticker + P (NVDAP, SPYP, BRKBP, GOOGLP; Visa → VP; Citi → CP) Sentinel & Symbology Card
Index / Mark SIP consolidated NBBO midpoint / median(last, bid, offer) on the contract's book Rules 43.8, 43.9
Funding Hourly: TWAP((Mark − Index)/Index) ÷ 24, locked at the top of the hour, final Rules 43.10, 43.11
Daily settlement VWAP of the Mark, 3:59:30–4:00:00 p.m. ET Rule 43.12
Hours SIP 23×5 tape: Sun 9:00 p.m. – Fri 8:00 p.m. ET, daily 8–9 p.m. break (from Dec 6, 2026) Rule 43.6
Halts / limits Halts with the stock (regulatory, LULD, market-wide); no daily price limit; 5% collar; orders within ±20% of prior settlement; max order 1,000 Rules 43.13, 43.14
Margin 15% federal floor (futures account); 20% FINRA 4210 or portfolio margin (securities account) Rule 3.3.2
Position limits 25,000 contracts, or accountability at 25,000; reportable at 1,000 Rules 43.18, 43.20
Blocks / EFP ECP-only blocks, minimum 15 contracts, reported within 15 minutes; EFP against the stock Rule 43.24
Clearing OCC on Ovation; funding paid inside daily variation (OCC Rules 501/502) Rule 43.11
Tax §1234B for customers; §1256(b)(1)(E), (g)(9) for dealers; no 60/40 claim Brief p4

Rule 43.7 defines profit and loss in one sentence: "the change in the Mark Price since the position was established, multiplied by the trading unit, plus or minus net Funding Adjustments." The eligible universe is the options-eligible universe, and settlement at OCC lets a firm hold the perpetual in the same margin account as the stock and its options.

The recommended launch fee is $1.50 per contract per side, or $0.40 for market makers. Funding is not a fee, and "The Exchange never takes any of it." The Section 31(d) security-futures assessment of $0.0042 per round turn is passed through.

Sources: MIAX Brief End-to-End (Sept 2026); Rulebook Insertion & Amendment Package v2.8 (Sep 30, 2026); Sentinel & Symbology Card v1.0 (Jul 7, 2026; rev. Sep 30, 2026); Runbooks v1.9 (Sep 17, 2026; rev. Sep 30, 2026)

The funding machine

Funding replaces expiry. Each hour, Onyx averages the gap between the contract and its stock and locks one rate. When the contract trades rich, longs pay shorts. When it trades cheap, shorts pay longs. The payment passes between holders, nets to zero and never touches the Exchange. The OCC Clearing Brief calls it the product's "enabling core, reusable across further perpetual classes to follow".

The Brief states the whole machine in six lines:

Line Formula Note
Index midpoint(consolidated NBBO) The stock, from the SIP
Mark median(Last, Bid, Offer) on the contract's own book The contract
Premium Mark − Index What funding disciplines
Funding Rate TWAP((Mark − Index) / Index) ÷ 24 Flat TWAP over the hour, 5-second samples; published = applied per-hour value; final at the lock
Funding Adjustment Funding Rate × Mark × net contracts × 100 Positive rate: longs pay, shorts receive
Mark (paused) Index + last valid Premium Nothing accrues while paused; an hour with no valid sample still prints its row (rate 0, status paused, final)

Sampling and the lock

Onyx samples the premium ratio every 5 seconds, about 720 times an hour. The rate is the plain equal-weight average of those samples, divided by 24 once, at the lock. The lock fires at :00 on the Onyx clock and is then final, with no bust and no true-up.

No other step divides, scales or converts the rate. Rule 43.10(d) says this applies to "the Exchange, the OCC, any OCC Clearing Member, or any other person". Every booking anywhere is rate × mark × contracts × 100. That makes the system an audit identity: a clearing firm can recompute its wire to the penny from public data.

A predicted rate ticks every 5 seconds and converges into the lock "with no jump". Because the average is flat, "Nothing happens at :00 for a quoter", which is why the design chose it over Kalshi's end-weighted "WAPI".

Two worked hours

  1. Headline case. The rate locks at +0.000042 per hour on a $250.00 Mark. A customer long 10 contracts books 0.000042 × 250 × 10 × 100 = $10.50, and the shorts receive $10.50. Over a 23-lock cycle this is about 0.097% per funded day. A funded day has 23 hours, never 24.
  2. From samples. In the 2–3 p.m. hour, 720 samples average +0.072%. Divided by 24, that gives +0.0030%. At a lock Mark of $250.25, 10 contracts pay $7.51.

The cycle and the pause

A Trading Day has 23 locks, from 22:00 through 20:00. The settlement cycle runs close-to-close, covering every lock after the prior 4:00 p.m. closing lock through today's.

  • A regular cycle has 23 rows: 4 evening, 7 overnight and 12 day.
  • An early-close cycle has 20 rows.
  • A name's first cycle has 19 rows.

The rule is "Build to the cycle, never to a constant."

Pause is the only non-normal state. It applies when the Index has no coverage or is halted, when Trading Status is Halt, Operational Halt or Closed, or when a Mark input is missing. During a pause the Mark rests at Index + last valid premium and nothing accrues. A partly live hour funds on its valid samples, however few. A fully paused hour still locks a row with rate 0. "A thin book is not a pause": there is no thin-book special case and no zero-rate fallback.

Who pays

Funding is struck at each lock instant, not at an end-of-day snapshot. A trade counts at a lock only if it executed strictly before HH:00:00.000. Execution times are truncated to the second, never rounded. A position opened and closed between two locks pays no funding. A position held across a lock pays the whole hour.

"Striking at the locks rather than end-of-day is what makes funding un-gameable: you cannot flatten a second before the close and pay nothing."— Unified Technical Specification, Vol. I, Jul 7, 2026 (rev. Sep 30, 2026)

Screens show funding per live hour. An annualized figure, if shown at all, is "hourly rate × funded hours per day × trading days per year (23 × 252). Never × 24 × 365". It is "Never the headline number." A customer's equity is cash + unrealized P&L + unrealized funding.

$10.50one worked hour of funding+0.000042/hr × $250 Mark × 10 contracts × 100. The ÷24 is applied once, at the lock; no divisor exists downstream.

Sources: MIAX Brief End-to-End (Sept 2026); OCC Clearing Brief (Sept 2026); Unified Technical Specification v1.0, Vol. I and Annex C (Jul 7, 2026; rev. Sep 30, 2026); Rulebook Insertion & Amendment Package v2.8 (Sep 30, 2026)

Index and Mark: the two prices

The Exchange computes nothing upstream of the Index. It is the midpoint of the SIP consolidated NBBO: CQS for Tapes A and B, UQDF for Tape C. A sample counts only if the quote is two-sided and not crossed. A locked quote counts. There is no staleness test. An invalid sample is excluded, and the last valid value is carried for display only. The Harmonization Submission calls this "reliability by exclusion, never fabrication". In code it is "one accumulator plus one valid-sample counter".

The Mark is the median of last trade, best bid and best offer on the contract's own book. It applies when the book is two-sided and a trade has printed. Otherwise the Mark is Index + last valid premium, which is zero before a name's first valid instant. Blocks and EFRPs are not inputs.

Feed health works as a halt. A tape outage pauses that tape's names, a full SIP outage pauses all of them, and a Level 3 market-wide halt closes the perpetual, with the remaining locks printing as paused zero rows.

MIAX Futures is the SIP subscriber of record. The Index is published free as derived data, midpoint and status only, never the underlying quotes. A non-distributed audit record keeps the bid and offer behind every Index sample for five years.

One record, three rails

Each lock writes one position-free record: lock time · rate · Mark · Index · Index status · Funding Status. The record goes out on three rails, "live off the feed, replay off the file":

  1. Message 18 "Funding Rate" on the Onyx ToM and DoM feeds: a predicted value every 5 seconds and a settled value at each :00.
  2. The public funding file onyx_financials_funding_rate_YYYYMMDD.csv. One file per cycle covers the whole class and sits on the Onyx Listings page. It is rewritten whole within seconds of each lock and never appended. It carries a running per-contract total, so a trade's share of the cycle is a single subtraction. This is the free "public historical-funding endpoint" of new Rule 6.21.
  3. The nightly furnish to OCC, sent once after the closing lock on the settlement-price rail, together with the daily settlement price and any dividend amount. "OCC never reads the feed."

The specification's six invariants come down to this: one record per lock, never absent; the pool sums to zero before money moves; cash is rounded once, at the wire. Rate, Mark and Index carry nine decimals. "Binary floating point never decides cash."

"'anyone can recompute the number' is worth more than a data fee — the crypto venues run the same free posture."— Unified Technical Specification, Vol. I, Jul 7, 2026 (rev. Sep 30, 2026)

Sources: Unified Technical Specification v1.0, Vol. I and Annex A (Jul 7, 2026; rev. Sep 30, 2026); Rule 41.23 Listing Submission, Index Source Appendix (draft, Sept 2026); Harmonization Submission (draft, Sept 2026)

Daily settlement and the money path

The funding rate is set every hour, but cash moves once a day, on rails OCC already runs.

The Daily Settlement Price (DSP) is "the volume-weighted average of the Mark over the half-open interval (3:59:30, 4:00:00 p.m. ET], computed from the six five-second sub-intervals". With no volume in the window, it is the simple average of the six Marks. On a half-day the window is 12:59:30–1:00:00 p.m. Two different numbers exist at 4:00 p.m. The 16:00 funding row uses the Mark at the instant of the lock, while the DSP is the 30-second windowed VWAP. The specification's instruction is "Never conflate them."

Annex C writes variation as 100 × ((DSP_today − DSP_prior) × q₀ + Σ (DSP_today − pⱼ) × Δqⱼ). Funding rides in the same daily payment as its own itemized line. Settlement is the next business day: members pay OCC by 9:00 a.m. ET and OCC pays members at 2:00 p.m. ET (OCC Rules 101, 501/502, 1301).

"the settlement is two lines, variation and funding, and never a third."— OCC Clearing Brief, Sept 2026

How OCC turns rows into cash

The Exchange furnishes position-free rows. OCC applies them to its finalized book. Only OCC can do this, because give-ups, allocations and transfers make the cleared book differ from the raw match. Each member's funding for a cycle, per OCC account, is:

start-of-cycle position × the cycle's per-contract total + Σ (each trade's signed quantity × the per-contract total of the locks after it).

Netting runs at two levels: first inside each FCM, then across clearing members at OCC, where the pool must equal zero at full precision. The check runs per name and per cycle, never across names. It holds by construction, because positions net to zero and every trade has two sides.

That check is "the one genuinely new control." If it fails, OCC corrects the position record and re-runs it. If it still fails, that name's cycle is held and paid later, never applied to later positions, while every other name settles. "No rate is re-made."

Give-ups move funding forward only. "OCC never re-allocates settled funding retroactively." Each account line is rounded once to the cent. The few cents by which the rounded lines miss zero stay with OCC, "the counterparty to every member."

"OCC computes the cash, not the rate."— OCC Clearing Brief, Sept 2026

Sources: OCC Clearing Brief (Sept 2026); Unified Technical Specification v1.0, Annexes B and C (Jul 7, 2026; rev. Sep 30, 2026); Runbooks v1.9, C1 and O2 (Sep 17, 2026; rev. Sep 30, 2026)

Margin: four layers, nothing net-new

"Nothing about the margin model is net-new." The perpetual inherits the OneChicago/OCC single-stock futures model in four layers.

Layer Set by What it is
A · Clearing margin OCC STANS: 10,000-scenario Monte Carlo, 99% expected shortfall, two-day margin period of risk (Rule 601); gross SPAN for segregated futures accounts
B · Customer parameters OCC MFE OFRA SPAN scan-range file, updated monthly
C · Regulatory floor CFTC + SEC 15% of current market value, initial and maintenance, since Dec 24, 2020 (17 CFR 41.45(b)(1) / 242.403(b)(1))
D · Portfolio margin OCC / FINRA Nightly RBH/CPM values (TIMS), one requirement across perp, stock and options; floor $37.50 per contract (FINRA 4210(g)(7)(B))

A customer's initial margin is "scan range % × underlying price × 100 shares". At 15% on a $75 stock, that is $1,125. The Exchange publishes no margin numbers. "No per-name leverage figure — leverage is broker-set."

The account type sets the floor. A futures account pays 15%. A securities account pays 20% under FINRA Rule 4210's strategy-based schedule unless it is portfolio-margined. Chapter V covers the ask to FINRA to close that gap. Market makers registered as Security Futures Dealers are margined house-side under Rule 3.3.2(e) once the SEC approves it.

Funding sits in neither margin number. It is "not a STANS or TIMS scenario input, not a customer-margin add-on; no funding-driven recalibration, exchange margin program or separate fund."

OCC's set-up work is to re-light the dormant security-futures scan class (discontinued Sept 18, 2020) and map each name into the class group of its stock and options.

Sources: OCC Clearing Brief (Sept 2026); Unified Technical Specification v1.0, Annex B §B.7 (Jul 7, 2026; rev. Sep 30, 2026); MIAX Brief End-to-End (Sept 2026)

Position limits and accountability

Limits are "two levels of one number". Every name is held at 25,000 contracts, either as a hard limit or as an accountability level, and a name moves between the two only when its data moves.

The federal regime (17 CFR 41.25(b)(3)) has three branches. The class uses two of them.

  • Default limit. At most 25,000 contracts net or same side, which is 2,500,000 shares. The figure was raised from 13,500 in 2019.
  • (B) accountability instead of a limit. This applies when six-month volume exceeds 2,500,000,000 shares and free float exceeds 40,000,000. A holder above 25,000 contracts must give information on request and stop increasing when ordered: "a large holder is seen rather than stopped."
  • (A) higher limit. Up to 12.5% of free float ÷ 100 contracts, where float exceeds 20,000,000 shares. This is reserved name by name and not adopted at launch.

Federal text applies limits in the last three trading days of an expiring month. A perpetual never expires, so the limit applies at all times (Rule 43.18). The playbook calls this "the more conservative reading, not a relaxation". The precedent is OneChicago's weekly futures, in a §41.24 submission dated Dec 20, 2013.

The reportable level is 1,000 contracts. Limits are reviewed every six months on June and December data, and a lowered limit must be met within 30 days. Hedge and spread exemptions are filed before exceeding and decided within 5 Business Days (Rule 43.25). A split that pushes a position over the limit is not a violation. "OCC does not enforce exchange limits; the Exchange does." FCMs load pre-trade controls from a machine-readable limits file, so "a change is a parameter update, not a build."

Applied to consolidated six-month data, the rules put 33 names on accountability and 68 on the 25,000-contract limit. SPCX, SKHY and CBRS start on the limit and are tested for (B) after six months of trading.

At August 26, 2026 prices, 25,000 contracts is worth about:

  • $93M of SMCI;
  • $108M of MIAX;
  • $111M of IBIT;
  • $528M of NVDA;
  • $4.36B of ASML.
25,000 contractsone number for every name33 names on accountability, 68 on the limit; the (A) branch up to 12.5% of float is reserved, not adopted.
Rivals carry 200,000-contract limits (CME, Coinbase, Bitnomial; Kalshi's cap); AP starts at the federal 25,000 and raises names only on data.
