X → ∞ · Chapter V

Chapter VIThe deal — sixty percent, held by paying

The deal is one master license with two numbers. American Perpetual LLC licenses everything Saheb built for U.S. equity perpetual futures to MIAX Futures Exchange, LLC. In return MIAX pays 60% of the transaction fees it actually collects on every perpetual product it or an affiliate ever lists, on any underlying, with no end date. It also pays $40,000,000 at signing, straight to Saheb. MIAX can never terminate the agreement. It keeps its exclusivity only for as long as it keeps paying.

The final text is dated 30 September 2026 and is ready to sign. Nothing in the record shows that it was signed, and on 16 October Saheb's own notes still treated it as unsigned.

This chapter covers the company, the agreement, the money, the math, the history from Greyline's 20%, the value, the tax, counsel's flags and where things stand.

60%of Covered Fees, flat, on every perpetual productNet only of the SEC's Section 31 assessment. No floor, no tiers, "never amended downward" (MPPLA ¶5(a)).
$40,000,000signing payment, paid to Saheb personallyNon-refundable, never credited against the royalty, outside every cap. The license takes effect only when he has received it (¶3(a), ¶5(h)).

The deal on one page

Everything fits on one page, by design. The Deal (10 pp, "FINAL", dated 30 September 2026) puts the two numbers on page one and the whole agreement behind them. The Master Perpetual Products License Agreement (MPPLA, "the master") v2.8 execution form is that same text, ready to sign.

Term What the final text says Clause
Parties American Perpetual LLC (Licensor) and MIAX Futures Exchange, LLC, a CFTC-registered designated contract market (DCM, a regulated futures exchange). Miami International Holdings, Inc. (MIH) acknowledges the guaranty Preamble, ¶5(i)
Royalty 60% of Covered Fees, paid quarterly in arrears within 30 days, with a statement per product and per venue ¶5(a)–(b)
Base Transaction fees actually collected, less rebates actually given, net only of Section 31 ¶1
Scope Every Perpetual Product on any underlying, on MIAX's or an affiliate's venues ¶1, ¶3(d)
Signing payment $40,000,000 on the Effective Date, payable to Saheb as assignee ¶5(h)
Term No end date. MIAX may never terminate ¶2, ¶9
Exclusivity U.S. DCMs and national securities exchanges. Released if nothing lists within 18 months, or if no royalty accrues for 12 straight quarters ¶3(a)
Launch cooperation Walkthroughs and explanations until first listing, unpaid ¶4(c)
AP's liability Capped at what MIAX paid in the 3 months before the first loss ¶8(c)
Guaranty MIH guarantees both payments, a guaranty of payment rather than collection, through bankruptcy ¶5(i)
Law Delaware, Court of Chancery, jury waiver ¶12

The Deal's ground rules list what is absent: "No minimums, no earn-outs, no equity, no services beyond the launch cooperation, no side letters."

The room keeps the numbers in one place. No rate appears outside page one of The Deal, Room Updates state no amount, and the planned email to Saheb's MIAX contact carries "no rate, no cash figure, no hash."

Sources: The Deal (30 Sep 2026); MPPLA v2.8 execution form (30 Sep 2026); PREPACK README v4.81 (30 Sep 2026)

American Perpetual LLC — the rent, not the building

The company is a single-purpose box: it owns the work and receives the royalty, and MIAX bears every operating and regulatory cost.

"This is the economics of an exchange without the exchange."— Deal, Structure & Closing Memo, 8 Jun 2026

Formation. Organized in Delaware on 1 June 2026 (file no. 10643461) by Nat Smith of Northwest Registered Agent Service, Inc.; registered office 8 The Green, Suite B, Dover, DE 19901. EIN letter 1 June; good standing 5 June. The formation operating agreement is an unsigned template; the organizer's resolutions adopted only "general provisions of an operating agreement" (F-34).

Ownership. Saheb is the sole member. The September papers recapitalize the company into 100,000,000 Units (1,000,000 = 1%) for one secondary sale of 10%, leaving Saheb 90,000,000. The June version used 36,000,000 Units, one per "royalty point" of the fee pool.

Governance after the amended and restated operating agreement (A&R OA).

  • Manager-managed, with Saheb as Manager and Partnership Representative.
  • Investors get negative consents (no debt, no new units, no sale of the royalty), most-favored-nation terms, anti-dilution, tag-along and a company right of first offer.
  • Saheb's own transfers are capped at 10,000,000 Units without investor consent (§5.5; 8,000,000 at v4.46).
  • Provider fees are capped at the greater of $250,000 a year or 25 bps of royalty received (§6.8).
  • Saheb carries the pre-royalty costs (§6.11) and may run other ventures, even competing ones (§9.2).

Money flows through, never pools.

  • The $40M never touches the company; the master assigns that receivable to Saheb (¶5(h)).
  • The royalty lands in a paying agent's collection account at a regulated bank or trust company (Computershare, Wilmington Trust or CSC class).
  • An administrator (Carta, Waystone or CSC class) runs a fixed waterfall: withholding, a small reserve, capped fixed costs, then pro-rata distribution. A CPA files the returns.
  • Saheb is nowhere in the cash path.
README_INSTRUMENTS says "the Company is member-managed: no Manager office"; the A&R OA and Investor Master make Saheb the Manager. Counsel must conform them (F-37).

Two smaller June-to-September mismatches: the Delaware annual tax ($300 vs $400) and distributions (monthly vs quarterly).

Sources: Initial Resolutions (1 Jun 2026); Schedule A Member Ledger (Sept 2026); A&R OA (Jun & Sept 2026); Counsel Document §05, §09 (28 Sep 2026); Investor Master §08–§10 (Sept 2026)

The master agreement, clause by clause

The master is written on Bloomberg Index Services' master-license form (public on EDGAR, the SEC's filing database), in the shape of MIAX's own Bloomberg index license. That makes it a commercial form MIAX has signed before. It departs from that form twice: it licenses a finished, delivered apparatus rather than an ongoing index service, and the royalty stream is "built to be owned."

What MIAX gets

MIAX gets an exclusive license to the "Originator Work": "product architecture, the funding methodology, the mark, settlement, and terminal-settlement design, the corporate-actions framework, the rulebook drafting, the technical specifications, and the regulatory work product" (Recital A). It may list on its Onyx platform and its affiliates' venues.

A product counts as a Perpetual Product by what it does, not what it is called. Any one of three tests is enough (¶1):

  1. It has no scheduled expiry, or one that rolls without a new trade.
  2. It uses a periodic funding, carry, premium, interest or reset payment that holds its price to the underlying. A funding rate is the periodic payment between longs and shorts that ties the perpetual to the stock.
  3. It is "designated, marketed or described as perpetual, continuous, rolling, open-ended" or an equivalent.

Options, spreads and strips with a perpetual leg are included. On venues a buyer of MIAX already ran, only contracts built on the Originator Work pay (added 28 Sept).

The base: Covered Fees

Covered Fees are "the transaction fees the Exchange and its Affiliates actually collect on Perpetual Products… less any rebate, discount, waiver, or promotional credit actually given… net only of the assessment under Section 31." They are never below zero.

No MIAX cost reduces them. ¶1 names market-data, data-license and index-source fees expressly. Saheb asked for that in his own words: "any like fee/license for like market data is covered not passed through to me."

The anti-impairment clause (¶5(g)) bars diverting fees to another revenue line, migrating or mirroring volume to shrink the royalty, and rebates that are not bona fide, published and open to all.

Term: perpetually bound

¶2 says "the Parties intend to be perpetually bound." The agreement never expires and never renews. Only American Perpetual can end it, and only in three cases:

  • MIAX's uncured material breach: 30 days to cure, 15 for an undisputed payment (¶9(a));
  • MIAX's insolvency (¶9(b));
  • no first listing within five years of signing, "until something is" (¶2(d)).

Every ending runs off: the royalty continues until open interest (the contracts still outstanding) is wound down (¶9(d)). MIAX's remedies against AP are injunction, specific performance and capped damages, "never termination, rescission, suspension or withheld payment."

"im not going to fire miax, im not going to end the deal, i dont want them to be able to end it either."— Saheb, PREPACK v4.76, 28 Sep 2026

Exclusivity, held by paying

Exclusivity covers U.S. DCMs and U.S. national securities exchanges. It starts only when Saheb receives the signing payment (¶3(a)). It releases to non-exclusive, with the license and the royalty unchanged, in two cases:

  • nothing lists within 18 months of signing (the "Longstop"). Delays by MIAX, OCC or regulators never extend it;
  • after launch, no royalty accrues for 12 consecutive calendar quarters.

The Investor Master calls the second test "three straight years." Non-U.S. venues of others, and non-exchange uses, stay free to AP. On 28 September Saheb also removed classes ("class concept does not exist"), so one exclusivity covers every perpetual product.

12 quartersno royalty for three straight years and exclusivity lapsesExclusivity is held by paying, not by listing. The 18-month Longstop covers the time before the first listing.

Launch cooperation: licensor, not staff

Saheb owes one bounded thing after delivery (¶4(c)): walkthroughs, agreed written clarifications, and factual explanations for MIAX's presentations to the SEC, CFTC, OCC and self-regulatory organizations. He gives them "reasonably… on notice," until the first listing and never beyond the Longstop. There is no pay and no hours cap, following the S&P–CME index-license form; MIAX's only remedy is specific performance.

He refused any employment role: "I won't be there for like 4 years to have everything vested. So like I don't want employment at all." The royalty does not depend on any of this.

"The Royalty follows the products, not the design."— MPPLA ¶4(b), 30 Sep 2026

Protections around the stream

  • Payments absolute. No set-off; audits up to twice a year; late interest WSJ prime + 2%; an underpayment over 2% puts the audit cost on MIAX; seven-year records (¶5(c), ¶5(e)).
  • Guaranty. MIH guarantees both payments for amounts accruing while MIAX is in its group, through any bankruptcy, a rejection included (¶5(i)).
  • Assignment. MIAX cannot assign without consent, and a merger is not an assignment. AP's side, and its equity, move freely (¶11). "Whoever owns MIAX, MIAX pays."
  • Confirmation. Within 10 business days, up to twice a year, MIAX confirms in writing to a buyer or lender AP names that the agreement is in force and the royalty paid (¶18).
  • Credit line. "American Perpetual LLC — originator and licensor," plus a disclaimer; MIAX may use the name in product names (¶6(d)). The May term sheet had gone further, with a class designation in the manner of "American" and "European" option styles.
Counsel's note N-2: an affiliate sold out of MIH's group "leaves the License, and its listings then pay nothing — the one gap left."

Sources: MPPLA v2.8 (30 Sep 2026); The Deal (30 Sep 2026); Counsel Document §03–§04 (28 Sep 2026); PREPACK v4.39–v4.81; Room Update 30 Sep 2026

The $40M — how the fixed cash moved

The fixed cash went from $1M at signing plus monthly availability fees to a single $40M payment in two weeks (11 to 25 September). The royalty went up at the same time. Each step also removed things: availability fees, a services schedule, later-class prices.

Date (cut) Fixed cash What sat beside it
Aug – 11 Sep $1,000,000 at signing + $500,000 at first listing $125,000/month founder availability (Launch Services Schedule)
14 Sep (v4.4) Not restated in the state note Further months of availability only by a "Months Instrument" at $83,333.33 each; later classes AP delivers paid by the 35% rate alone
18 Sep (Deal Sept ed.) $5,000,000 at signing $1,000,000 founder availability over 12 months, paid monthly in advance
24 Sep (v4.39) $6,000,000 at signing Nothing. "no availability"
24 Sep (v4.42) $10,000,000 at signing Nothing ("Change to 10m")
25 Sep (v4.55) $40,000,000 at signing Nothing. Nothing at first listing

The $6M equals the $5M plus the $1M of the 18 September edition, folded into one payment. Saheb's instruction for that cut was:

"60 and 6m, one royalty for all perpetuals. no availability hand over yeah agree, licensor not staff. Royalty no floor."— Saheb, PREPACK v4.39, 24 Sep 2026

The jump to $40M came the next day, and his reason was MIAX's market value:

"i need 40m signing bonus, if you see the perp futures announcement like the market dropped 15% on all exchange stocks … the stock will climb more than 40m in market cap on the announcement of the product."— Saheb, PREPACK v4.55, 25 Sep 2026

The room checked his claim and printed what the articles said, not 15%. On 2 June 2026, as markets absorbed the CFTC's approval of the first crypto perpetual, Cboe fell 9% in a session and CME, ICE and Nasdaq fell 4–5%. The Deal sets $40M beside MIAX's value: "about one percent of MIAX's market capitalization" (≈$3.9B in mid-September).

Three mechanics make the $40M Saheb's alone. The receivable is irrevocably assigned to him inside the master (¶5(h)). Line 2 of the Payment Direction pays it to his own account and cannot change without him. And the license takes effect only on his receipt in full (¶3(a)). The Tax Document's one rule: "The signing payment is the Founder's and the royalty is the Company's, and the two never mix."

AP's liability cap moved three times: "greater of the Signing Payment and 12 months" (v4.46), then all amounts paid for a title breach (v4.77), then three months' payments (v4.78). The prepack expects MIAX's counsel to raise the three-month cap.

Sources: PREPACK v4.4, v4.39, v4.42, v4.55, v4.77–v4.78; Room Update 18 Sep 2026; AP Data Room Final Check (25 Sep 2026); The Deal p2

The signing set and the delivery record

The close is designed as one sitting. The founder signs, then MIAX Futures, then MIH, and the wire follows. "One agreement. One signing. Thirty days." The target is "signed within thirty days of the yes."

Before the envelope. Saheb executes the IP Assignment (moving all the work from him to the company) and the sole member's written consent. MIAX's counsel receives copies, a fresh Delaware good-standing certificate and both W-9s. MIAX names its signatories and fills its notice blocks; AP fills the Payment Direction's two account lines.

The Signing Set. Four papers, eight pages, one PDF:

Paper Pages What it does Who signs
1 Master (MPPLA v2.8) 1–5 The whole deal, ¶¶1–19 AP · MIAX Futures · MIH acknowledging ¶5(i)
2 Delivery Acknowledgment 6 Records what was delivered, against the hashed manifest AP · MIAX Futures
3 Payment Direction 7 Royalty → company collection account; $40M → Saheb AP · Saheb · MIAX acknowledging
4 Guaranty 8 MIH guarantees every payment MIH · AP accepting

Every line carries a tag such as SIGN HERE (AP) ▸, so DocuSign AutoPlace places the fields itself: AP 4 signatures, the founder 1, MIAX Futures 3, MIH 2.

The delivery record. The consideration is a recorded delivery, not a promise.

  • The Delivery Acknowledgment lists 40 delivered items and quotes the SHA-256 hash (a file fingerprint) of the manifest AP_MIAX_Delivery_Manifest_2026-09-30.csv: 99 files, hash 8d449cd9…8bc3c. The manifest (path, version date, size, hash per file) governs what was delivered.
  • "Expressly not part of this delivery": AP's later work on any underlying.
  • What MIAX actually holds is the 28 September cut: v4.71, 93 files, hash 354901985d…6339.

The wire rule. Instructions are verified by live callback to a known number; an emailed change of account is never acted on; bank details appear only in the Payment Direction.

Sources: README_INSTRUMENTS (30 Sep 2026); AP_MIAX_Signing_Set (30 Sep 2026); AP Delivery Acknowledgment form (30 Sep 2026); The Deal part 05; PREPACK v4.81

Fees: 0.63 basis points

The royalty is a share of fees MIAX sets, so the room proposes the fees as well. The Fee and Revenue Exhibit prices the pool and "deliberately" does not price the split. Fees stay "MIAX's alone" (¶5(g)).

The launch schedule is flat: $1.50 per contract per side, and $0.40 for a market maker. The realized contract is ≈$33,600 of notional (100 shares at the listing group's volume-weighted $335.82). At a 60/40 mix of regular and market-maker sides, a round turn collects ≈$2.12, or ≈0.63 basis points (bps, hundredths of a percent) of everything that trades.

0.63 bpsthe launch fee pool as a share of traded notional≈1.0 bp if MIAX adopts the optional tier schedule. Every $10B a day of trading ≈ $160M a year of pool at launch, ≈$250M at the tier schedule.

The corridor. The field runs from $0.35 to $10.00 a side. The recommended band, $1.25–$2.00, is where five tests overlap: above commoditized beta, a novelty premium, customer all-in at about half the offshore fee, the median name, and a market-maker rate near 24–30% of regular. The hard floor is $0.50 ($0.10 for market makers); the recommended ceiling $5.00; the hard ceiling $10.00.

The optional tier schedule steps the fee by share price and is re-bucketed every six months:

Price bucket Names Regular / side Market maker / side
Under $50 24 $0.50 $0.15
$50–100 43 $1.00 $0.30
$100–200 49 $1.75 $0.50
$200–500 63 $2.25 $0.60
$500+ 21 $2.75 $0.75

The realized blend is ≈$1.73 a side, ≈1.0 bp. The Fee Exhibit calls that +63% over launch; the Investor Master says ≈+59%.

Against the field. All-in customer cost is ≈$2.07–$3.10 a side (≈0.83–1.24 bps) on a $250 name, against ≈4.5–5.5 bps for offshore base takers. The room's own multiple varies: "4–7× cheaper," "4–6× under" and "~5× under" all appear. CME's dated single-stock future charges $0.60 a side.

Sources: Fee and Revenue Exhibit v1.1 (Sept 2026); MIAX Brief End to End §11 (Sept 2026); Investor Master §06, §12 (Sept 2026); How to Win Part 02 (Sept 2026)

The revenue grid

The grid prices the royalty on one reference: the top 200 U.S. stocks and ETFs by trailing notional. That "listing group" turns over $147.8T a year in the stock itself, or $589B a day. The year-one objective is $20–40T of perpetual notional, 14–27% of that tape.

Annual notional Share of group tape Pool, launch Pool, tier Royalty at 60% (launch → tier)
$10T 7% $630M $1.00B $378M – $600M
$20T 14% $1.26B $2.00B $756M – $1.20B
$40T 27% $2.52B $4.00B $1.51B – $2.40B
$80T 54% $5.04B $8.00B $3.02B – $4.80B
$148T (parity) 100% $9.32B $14.8B $5.59B – $8.88B

The sources round differently. The Fee Exhibit puts $10T at $631M launch and $1.03B tier, and $40T at $2.52B launch and $4.12B tier. The Investor Master's tier column uses exactly 1.0 bp.

The split at $10T. Of ≈$630M of fees, ≈$378M a year goes to American Perpetual and ≈$252M stays with MIAX. The Deal values MIAX's share at "≈$1.7B at MIAX's mid-September trading multiple," about 6.9× net revenue. That is more than forty times the signing payment. The page-one figure was $1.8B at an earlier cut.

$378M a yearAP's royalty if the class trades $10T, about 7% of the stocks' own tapeMIAX keeps ≈$252M. At the tier schedule the same volume pays AP ≈$600M.

The far edge. AP earns ≈$10B a year only when the equity class clears ≈$167T a year at the tier schedule, or ≈$265T at launch: 17–26% of the room's ≈$1Q (one quadrillion dollars) mature estimate. The four-class figure, ≈$1.55Q, is labeled a ceiling, "not projections."

MIAX's live fee schedule has a $0.00 retail tier. The 25 Sept Final Check found that if half the full-rate volume in the $10T case paid $0, AP's royalty would fall from ~$378M to ~$218M. The 14 Sept "greater-of" base guarded against that; the 24 Sept cut dropped it.

The text's answer is ¶5(g)(iii): rebates and promotions must be bona fide, published and open to all similarly situated. Counsel flag F-32 asks whether that and ¶1 "read as one test." A published zero-fee tier is the case to test it against.

Sources: Investor Master §06, §12 (Sept 2026); Fee and Revenue Exhibit p8–p10 (Sept 2026); The Deal p2 (30 Sep 2026); AP Data Room Final Check (25 Sep 2026)

Sixty percent against the benchmarks

Sixty percent is far above what index licensors take, and the room says so openly. Its defense is that AP delivered the whole apparatus, not a name.

Comparable Disclosed Effective take
Cboe–S&P (SPX options) $233.3M royalty fees and other cost of revenues ÷ 1.03B index-option contracts (2024) ≈$0.227 a contract, at most ≈25% of Cboe's ≈$0.905 revenue per contract
S&P DJI–CME (S&P 500 futures) S&P DJI usage-based royalties $267M (2023 10-K) ≈$0.18–0.21 a contract, ≈20–30% of CME's per-contract revenue (estimate)
SPY 3 bps of ≈$800B AUM plus a fixed fee ≈a third of the fund's 9.45 bp expense ratio
"Index licensors take a fifth to a third — for a name. This delivers the apparatus."— MIAX Brief End to End p18, Sept 2026

The GLX-era research had triangulated lower, converging on "~25–35% of op profit ≈ ~15–25% of revenue," with a 50% ceiling at Hyperliquid's HIP-3. The two sets disagree on S&P DJI–CME: 20–30% in the Brief, ~15–19% in the GLX Fee Architecture work.

The case for 60% is what MIAX does not have to build: every filing drafted, the rulebook chapter, the 124-page specification, the OCC clearing plan, the corporate-actions apparatus and the street build. The case against it is MIAX's 40%, out of which MIAX bears every operating, regulatory and clearing cost.

60% leaves MIAX 40% of the fees while the room's own benchmarks put licensors at 20–33%. This is the main negotiation risk, and no document records MIAX's view of it.

Saheb's own framing on 28 September was about durability, not the split: "Tell me how to make the contract the best paper. Really personally I don't care it's Miax / Like I'm only doing this thru/with them."

Sources: MIAX Brief End to End §13 (Sept 2026); GLX Fee Architecture Reference (undated, GLX era); PREPACK v4.77 (28 Sep 2026); notes 01_AP_core and 07_GLX_A

From 20% to 60% — how the deal was cut

The licensing idea was re-cut about eighty times, and the rate tripled. Two other things moved with it: the base shifted from a "gross fee pool" to fees actually collected, and approval risk moved off the buyer, because units now sell only after clearance.

Stage Date Royalty and base Fixed cash Term and exclusivity
Greyline as introducing broker Mar–Apr 2026 90% of own flow + 20% origination royalty on everyone else's — —
GLX, licensor only ~May 2026 Band ~30–36% of gross pool; then 36% (split 40 integrators / 36 GLX / 24 MIAX) — Perpetual
MIAX Binding Term Sheet (unsigned) May 2026 36% of "Gross Fee Pool," monthly — 15-yr initial + 5-yr renewals while open interest exists; U.S. DCMs only
Silver Lake package 5–8 Jun 2026 Same 36 points Sale of 3.5 points for $210M Same
MPPLA v0.9 working copy 15 Jul 2026 Product Schedules, Rate Amendment Instrument, Class ADV, floors — —
Launch Services era Aug – 11 Sep "45% of Covered Fees" in 3 Sept runbook sources; greater-of base $1M + $500K + $125K/month —
Deal v3 / Sept ed. 17–18 Sep 45% equity for life; 35% / 25% later lines $5M + $1M availability 10 yrs from first listing; per-class exclusivity
Deal v4 24 Sep 60% of fees collected, net of §31, no floor $6M → $10M 10 yrs renewing; held by listing
Signing payment 25 Sep 60% $40M Same
No end date 28 Sep (v4.75–v4.80) 60%, one exclusivity, no classes $40M Perpetual; held by paying; 3-month cap
Final 30 Sep (v4.81) MPPLA v2.8 $40M Same

The May term sheet

The unsigned MIAX Binding Term Sheet ("Dated as of May ___, 2026") was a preliminary agreement in two parts, citing Teachers v. Tribune and SIGA v. PharmAthene: binding terms, plus a duty to negotiate the rest in good faith.

Its royalty was 36% of a Gross Fee Pool defined as "the single pool of revenue from which Originator, Exchange, and all clearing members are paid." The final text dropped its other terms: a joint SEC/CFTC "Petition" for exemptive relief; a no-file covenant with liquidated damages of 100% of a similar product's gross revenue for 36 months; audits at SOFR + 4%; AP's right to accelerate the royalty's present value on a MIAX change of control; securitization rights naming sovereign wealth funds; and the "American Perpetual" class designation.

The Silver Lake package (June)

The June package was a fully drafted Phase-One closing for "Silver Lake LTC" (the meaning of "LTC" is never stated). Saheb would sell 3.5 of 36 royalty points (3,500,000 Units, 9.7222%) for $210,000,000, or $60,000,000 a point, to a Silver Lake–designated Cayman Islands vehicle wiring him directly. He would keep 32,500,000 Units (90.2778%). Distributions were monthly, and the rail was "bankruptcy-remote and built to be banked or securitized." The buyer took the approval risk at a discount.

$210,000,000June price for 3.5 of 36 royalty points ($60M a point)Implies ≈$2.16B for the whole 36-point royalty (36 × $60M). The GLX Investor Document had offered the same block at $30M per half-point.
"Whether SEC/CFTC approval comes is the bet you are knowingly making, at a discounted price."— Deal, Structure & Closing Memo, 8 Jun 2026

No executed document shows that Silver Lake engaged. The September papers drop the name and genericize the buyer.

September: the OCC-era re-cut

The product itself changed under the deal:

June design September design
Clearing VeriClear / "MIAX Futures Clearing" OCC (the Options Clearing Corporation)
Contract 1 share 100 shares
Fees Basis points Dollars per side
Dividends Unadjusted Adjusted by rule

The deal followed. Saheb's lines from 24–28 September read as a spec: "i want forever"; "no availability"; "licensor not staff"; "class concept does not exist"; "i dont want them to be able to end it either." The research behind "perpetual" (28 Sept) found that a royalty on unpatented work can run as long as the products sell (Aronson v. Quick Point; Brulotte and Kimble reach only patents).

Sources: MIAX Binding Term Sheet (May 2026); Deal, Structure & Closing Memo (8 Jun 2026); Member Ledger (6 Jun 2026); PREPACK v4.4–v4.81; Room Updates 18, 28, 30 Sep 2026; notes 07_GLX_A, 08_GLX_B

What the stream is worth

The Investor Master values the company like a licensing franchise, at a reference band of 15–25× the royalty. What is for sale is fixed: one sale of 10% of the company, to one buyer, at Saheb's discretion, with no posted price ("your price for the 10%").

Comparables. Index licensors and exchanges trade at ≈18–30× earnings, precious-metal royalty companies at 27–34×. Kalshi was valued at $22B in May, with talks at $40B, on about $264M of 2025 fees; the royalty "carries none" of an exchange's costs.

The ladder (tier fee state; launch runs ≈0.63× of these):

Level AP royalty / yr Company at 15–25× Cash to the 10% / yr The 10% at 15–25×
$20–40T (year-one objective) $1.2–2.4B ≈$18–60B ≈$120–240M ≈$1.8–6.0B
$80T (half of parity) $4.8B ≈$72–120B ≈$480M ≈$7.2–12B
$148T (parity) $8.88B ≈$133–222B ≈$888M ≈$13.3–22.2B
≈$1Q (crypto parity) $60.0B ≈$900B–1.5T ≈$6.0B ≈$90–150B
$13.3–22.2Bthe 10% at parity, the row Saheb prices fromParity means the perpetual trades as much as the 200 stocks themselves, $148T a year. Year one, the 10% references ≈$1.8–6.0B.

Three paths give the 10% five-year cumulative cash of:

Path Volume, years 1 · 3 · 5 Five-year cash to the 10%
Slow $20T · $40T · $80T ≈$1.38B
Base $30T · $80T · $148T ≈$2.56B
Crypto-like $40T · $150T · $450T ≈$6.21B

At parity the 10% yields 4–7% on a parity-row price. On the crypto ratio it yields 12–20%, and 27–45% at crypto parity. That fits "permanent capital with a long horizon and an 8–10% return requirement — a sovereign, a large family office, an insurer." It does not fit a five-year fund with a 15% hurdle.

The payor. MIH reported $660.5M cash, $1.5M of debt and $141M quarterly net revenue at a 54% adjusted-EBITDA margin for Q2 2026, and was worth ≈$3.9B in mid-September.

Why sell at all.

"I think I can sell 6% of my 60% royalty, so 10% of the entity. It's hard to have 6% be the like number in a serious sale."— Saheb, PREPACK v4.72, 28 Sep 2026

Earlier cuts spoke of "one sale of 6%" and a 0.5%-block ladder. The final reason is stated plainly: Saheb "wants liquidity, and he is willing to sell duration for it." The sale happens before the royalty pays or not at all, and "the seller keeps nine times what he sells."

Under MIPA §8(j)–(k), no unit sells until the royalty agreement is executed and the SEC/CFTC clearances have issued. The MIPA's indemnity has a 0.5% basket, a 10% cap (the price, for fundamental representations), fraud uncapped, and no set-off against Saheb's retained distributions (§7.6).

June valued the whole royalty at ≈$2.16B with the buyer carrying approval risk; September's ladder puts the company at ≈$18–60B at the year-one objective, sold only after approval. They price different risks, and neither is a market price.

Sources: Investor Master §07–§08 (Sept 2026); Counsel Document §10 (28 Sep 2026); MIPA form §7–§8 (Sept 2026); PREPACK v3.6–v3.7, v4.65, v4.72

Tax

The Tax Document covers two taxpayers and four money events, and its answer for Saheb is ordinary income throughout. The September view reverses the June memo, which had assumed capital gain.

The four events.

  1. IP Assignment. No federal tax while the company is a disregarded single-member LLC; unpaid time creates no basis.
  2. The $40M. Ordinary income in the year of the wire: a license, not a sale, of self-created work (§1221(a)(3)); no patent, so no §1235. Either self-employment tax (≈$1,420,000) or the 3.8% net investment income tax (≈$1,520,000) applies, never both. On wire day, set aside 37% federal + 3.8% + state and make estimated payments; it is reported in 1099-MISC box 2.
  3. The royalty. On Saheb's return while the company is disregarded; once a buyer is admitted, investment income to the partners (Higgins), with 30% or treaty withholding for foreign members (§1441).
  4. The 10% sale. Also ordinary (Rev. Rul. 99-5; Hort; P.G. Lake). The buyer amortizes over 15 years (§197).
The June memo told Saheb "roughly 23.8% federal plus your state" on a ~$210M capital gain. The September Tax Document says ordinary income on both the $40M and any unit sale.

What the U.S. takes per $1 of a buyer's royalty (Investor Master §09A): nothing from a U.S. tax-exempt; 0–15¢ from treaty investors (0¢ most of Europe, Japan, Canada; 15¢ Korea, India, Israel); 21¢ of the net from U.S. corporations and 41¢ from U.S. individuals, near zero for 15 years after §197 amortization at the ladder's multiples; 30¢ of the gross from non-treaty foreigners and sovereigns investing directly (§892 does not reach royalties). A royalty-note structure was rejected (§871(h)(4)).

The §871(m) note. §871(m) is the withholding rule for dividend equivalents paid to foreign holders, and a single-stock perpetual falls squarely under it. The product resolves this by construction: before each ex-date the exchange reports a per-share distribution amount, OCC lowers the prior settlement price by it, and that published figure is the withholding base ("base = factor × 100 × contracts"). Withholding becomes turnkey for the carrying firm. "It makes §871(m) clean" matters to the deal because foreign distribution is volume, and volume is royalty.

One tax point stays open for every carrying firm: how the hourly Funding Adjustment itself is treated.

Sources: Tax Document v1 (28 Sep 2026); Investor Master §09, §09A (Sept 2026); Deal, Structure & Closing Memo §9 (8 Jun 2026); notes 01_AP_core, 04_AP_clearing

Counsel's flags

The Counsel Document (28 Sept, v1) briefs engaged transaction counsel. It is written as if MIAX had already said yes: "The deal is agreed. Three phases make it paid."

It sets three phases: get the deal final (IP Assignment, verify what MIAX signs, close); stand up the royalty rail; and finalize the closing set, held until the product is cleared to list. Counsel's fees are fixed, capped by phase and deferred to signing, paid out of the signing payment, with a modest floor fee if nothing signs. The mandate: "The Founder signs, decides, and answers questions — nothing else."

The register. The printed register carries 24 flags, numbered between F-1 and F-42. The ones that carry the deal:

Flag The call Posture
F-1 The agreed numbers 60 flat · $40M · no end date · 18-month Longstop · 3-year no-royalty release · 5-year end (AP's)
F-4 Exclusivity scope U.S. DCMs + national securities exchanges; everything else free to AP
F-7 ¶16 investor-diligence carve-out Must survive MIAX review; fallback clean team or NDA, never deletion
F-29 MIH guaranty Absolute, until MIH's group disposes of MIAX
F-30 Liability cap Three months' payments, Bloomberg's measure. Confirm
F-31 No end date Pepsi Cola (2d Cir. 2020), Burford (7th Cir. 2015), Zaha Hadid (EWCA 2026); a patent stays outside (Brulotte, Kimble)
F-32 Covered Fees vs rebates Do ¶1 and ¶5(g)(iii) read as one test?
F-33 Payment Direction royalty line Open the collection account before signing, or complete line 1 later
F-35, F-37, F-39, F-40 Closing-set drafting Units left "[#]"; Partnership Representative mismatch; no transfer-at-death lane; no Buyer indemnity
F-41 "The Works" Reaches any pre-formation perpetual work. Confine it if intended
F-42 §704(c) method Traditional (ceiling rule) limits the buyer's amortization; remedial gives full share

F-41 and the five ventures Saheb once kept

The September IP Assignment sweeps in more than the June one did. Its ¶2 defines "the Works" as all work product Saheb "has created or creates, before or after the Company's formation, for exchange-traded perpetual products or the business of the Company." In ¶6(d) he represents that he "retains no project or work product related to the Company's business outside this Assignment." His 25 September disclosures read "none" for contractors, prior employment, liens, retained IP and domains.

The June IP Assignment did the opposite. Its Schedule B "Retained IP" kept five future ventures for Saheb:

  1. an FCM or broker-dealer, including a dual-registered FCM-BD (an FCM, or futures commission merchant, is a futures broker that holds customer funds);
  2. a customer-facing perpetual and derivatives trading app;
  3. a white-label integration platform, turnkey distribution package or SaaS for carrying firms;
  4. an operating company to acquire, own or operate a DCM or DCO (a clearinghouse) other than MIAX;
  5. trading strategies, quantitative methods, and arbitrage and market-making methods.

That list mirrors GLX's May 2026 plan to buy MIAX Futures' DCM and DCO, the Dorman FCM and Connamara. Under the room's rule "No earlier venture named" (Saheb: "Absolutely 0"), seven archive files named for the earlier ventures were moved out of the room on 28 September.

The A&R OA's §9.2 still lets Saheb run competing ventures. The MPPLA's ¶1, however, reaches any perpetual on any underlying that MIAX lists.

"Carve Rei out of the American Perpetual deal and its IP assignment before either is signed. As drafted, a Rei perp at MIAX owes AP 60% of its fees and falls under AP's exclusivity." — Rei × MIAX— The Brief, private to-dos, 16 Oct 2026
The September IP Assignment and the "any underlying" definition would capture work Saheb plans elsewhere (the June carve-outs, GLX-era material, Rei's crypto perps). F-41 is the place to decide what stays outside.

Sources: Counsel Document §01, §07–§12 (28 Sep 2026); IP Assignment execution form (28 Sep 2026); June IP Assignment, Schedule B (Jun 2026); A&R OA §9.2; Rei × MIAX — The Brief (16 Oct 2026); notes 08_GLX_B

Did MIAX sign?

The record shows no signature, and the October evidence points to none yet.

  • MIAX's access. Joe Ferraro, President of MIAX Products, has had the Drive link since 15 July, refreshed 19 August and 18 September. The Room Update of 28 September (v4.71, 93 files) went at 11:43 a.m. ET that Monday. The Deal in that cut carries 60% and $40M, but ten years from first listing and exclusivity held by listing.
  • The 30 September cut. The no-end-date, held-by-paying text (v4.75–v4.81) is ready. The prepack's last word: "Nothing sent."
  • The call agendas. The 28 September Room Update is a seven-item agenda; the 30 September one has five items and refers to "the few things from Monday," so either the call moved or there were two. Its first item asks for "counsel's read & path to signing."
  • The six asks (Brief §08A). Three answers: confirm MIAX Futures' Section 6(g) securities registration under its current name (or file a fresh Form 1-N); lead a written amendment of the OCC clearing agreement recording that funding is variation (a daily margin payment); propose full-session clearing at OCC. Three papers: OCC's restricted trade-source guides, the B500 Market Maker Program terms, and the papers MIAX sends market makers.
  • The Counsel Document. Its "MIAX has agreed to the one-page commercial terms" describes the moment it is meant for: "The Founder approaches counsel only once MIAX has agreed." No engagement is recorded.
  • October. Saheb last viewed the INVESTORS master on 12 October and the MIAX folder on 22 October. On 16 October his Rei brief planned to carve Rei out "before either is signed," and noted that MIAX was not among the September Form 1-N filers.
The Counsel Document (28 Sept) says "the deal is agreed"; the prepack (30 Sept) says "Nothing sent" and MIAX still owes three answers and three papers; the Rei brief (16 Oct) treats the deal as unsigned. No document records MIAX's yes.

The prepack's next steps.

  1. Fill MIAX's signatories, notice blocks and the Payment Direction account lines; re-make the PDFs; re-bind the Signing Set.
  2. Prepare the send in conversation, not in the room: the folder changes against v4.71, the Drive steps, the email to his MIAX contact. "Every send is his."
  3. Share by audience. MIAX gets its folder after his approval; counsel gets all three folders only on MIAX's yes; investors get nothing "until both signatures"; never a common Drive root.
  4. Decide his own items: the two "best paper" changes (held by paying; the ¶18 confirmation), the Kalshi withholding line, the Fee Exhibit's promotions sentence, the "framework" small print, the buyer and price for the 10%, and page one's $1.7B MIAX figure.

Waiting on others: MIAX's six asks; OCC's two points (no-expiry dates on its systems, clearing through the full session); FINRA conforming Rule 4210 from 20% to the federal 15% margin; counsel's flags F-30, F-31 and F-33–F-42; and a 15 U.S.C. 78q-1(b)(7)(B) point before the §6(h)(7)(C) compliance date.

X → ∞ · Chapter VI

Chapter VIIThe street — market makers, brokers and the app

A listed perpetual is only as real as the firms that can carry it. American Perpetual answers "who has to build what" in one sentence, and the partner documents repeat it: every firm that carries the contract "builds the same thing: four inputs, four booking rules, one screen, one report line." The exchange computes the rate, OCC moves the money, and the firm books one line and shows it.

This chapter covers the street layer of the September 2026 data room: the Launch Partner Brief, the clearing-member annex, margin as a customer meets it, the Market Maker Program, the Robinhood brief and its four app screens, the Connamara option and the public site. It then turns to the competitors that appeared between CME's relaunch on July 27, 2026 and Robinhood's crypto-perp announcement on September 29, 2026.

Everything here is prepared, and none of it is engaged. MIAX Futures has not engaged an integration partner. Robinhood has agreed to nothing. Every handout says "Products are subject to regulatory approval and are not currently available for trading."

The briefs and who reads them

AP wrote one brief per audience. All of them draw on one technical specification (UTS v1.0, revised September 30, 2026), so no firm has to work anything out for itself. "Training is reading, not discovery."

Document Reader Date Length What it asks for
Launch Partner Brief v2.4 FCMs, clearing members, market makers Sept 2026 24 pp (28 bound) An NDA, a conformance slot, a switch
UTS Annex C — FCM/CM Build Member engineering and operations July 7, rev. Sept 30, 2026 12 pp The whole member build
UTS Annex D — Public Site MIAX web and product teams July 7, rev. Sept 30, 2026 3 pp Naming, notices, downloads, copy rules
Fact Sheet; Contract Specifications Customers 2026 2 pp each —
Market Maker Brief Market makers, principal trading groups Sept 2026 12 pp Names you would make, a contact, an NDA
Robinhood Brief Robinhood Markets, Inc. Sept 2026 edition 14 pp (18 bound) An NDA, two notices, a screen, a switch
Connamara Integration Brief Connamara Systems (Daniel Curry, CRO) Sept 2026 25 pp An NDA and a working session
Street Build memo MIAX Products Leadership Sept 16, conformed Sept 28, 2026 5 pp A yes or no on one outside team

Authorship is layered on purpose. The Launch Partner Brief, Market Maker Brief, Fact Sheet and Contract Specifications carry "© Miami International Holdings". Each reads "FROM: MIAX Futures, with American Perpetual LLC (Originator)". The Robinhood Brief is "© 2026 American Perpetual LLC".

AP drafted the partner materials in the Exchange's voice so MIAX could adopt them as its own. After signing, "the work & every contact are the Exchange's". The briefs go out first, and the mutual NDA then opens Annex C. AP "keeps no operational role."

Every partner brief speaks for MIAX, but the room shows MIAX issuing none of them. Until signing they are AP's drafts.

The Robinhood Brief's version label is inconsistent. The September 18 room update calls it v1.9 and the folder guide calls it v2.0. The text itself says "September 2026 edition".

Sources: Launch Partner Brief v2.4 (Sept 2026); UTS Annex C, D (July 7, rev. Sept 30, 2026); Market Maker Brief (Sept 2026); Robinhood Brief (Sept 2026); Connamara Integration Brief (Sept 2026); Room Updates (Sept 18, 28, 30, 2026)

The Launch Partner Brief

The Launch Partner Brief is the document a carrying firm takes "to the meeting upstairs". It covers three things:

  • The product: "a no-expiry, cash-settled future on 100 shares of a single U.S. equity — held to its stock by an hourly funding rate, listed on MIAX Futures, cleared at OCC, carried in the accounts your customers already have."
  • The build: "Four booking rules. One added line on your OCC report."
  • The business: one cleared book for the whole street, and a member build done once that carries every later class.

The cover makes the demand case in four numbers:

  • perpetuals traded more than $200B a day offshore in 2025;
  • they are more than 90% of global crypto-futures volume;
  • the top 200 U.S. names' own cash tape trades $589B a day;
  • more than 100 OCC clearing members are "already in place".

The machine in six lines

Line Formula What it means
Index midpoint(consolidated NBBO) The SIP quote midpoint, used only when two-sided and not crossed
Mark median(Last, Bid, Offer) The contract's own price on its own book
Premium Mark − Index "What funding disciplines"
Funding Rate TWAP((Mark − Index)/Index) ÷ 24 Flat average of 5-second samples (~720 an hour), final at the lock
Funding Adjustment Rate × Mark × net contracts × 100 Positive rate: longs pay, shorts receive
Mark (paused) Index + last valid Premium Only an hour with no valid sample locks at rate 0

The funding rate is the hourly payment between longs and shorts that ties the perp to its stock. It is uncapped ("no clamp on the rate"). What contains it sits upstream: a 5% trading collar, the median Mark and the hour-long average.

In the brief's worked hour, the rate is +0.000042 on a $250.00 Mark, about 0.097% per funded day. A holder long 10 contracts pays 0.000042 × 250 × 10 × 100 = $10.50, and the shorts receive $10.50.

Contract terms.

  • Size and tick: 100 shares; $0.01 a share, or $1.00 a contract.
  • Hours: Sunday 9:00 p.m. to Friday 8:00 p.m. ET, with a daily 8–9 p.m. break. That gives 23 funded hours a day, "never twenty-four". The SIP's 23×5 production starts December 6, 2026.
  • Daily settlement: the volume-weighted Mark over 3:59:30–4:00:00 p.m. ET. Cash moves through OCC the next business day: members pay by 9:00 a.m. ET and OCC pays out by 2:00 p.m.
  • Margin and limits: a 15% margin floor; a 25,000-contract position limit or accountability level; reporting at 1,000 contracts.

Fees and the all-in cost

The recommended launch schedule is $1.50 per contract per side, flat, and $0.40 for market makers. Price-bucket tiers are "the named evolution".

Funding is never a fee: "it passes between position holders and nets to zero; the venue collects nothing on it."

With a typical FCM commission and the pass-throughs, a customer pays about 0.83–1.24 bps a side on a $250 name. Offshore base takers pay about 4.5–5.5 bps, "before the offshore venue's own spread costs".

The pass-throughs are small:

  • SEC §31: $0.0042 per round turn.
  • NFA: $0.01 a side, in futures accounts only.
  • FINRA TAF: $0.000135 a contract, paused for October–December 2026.
4–7×cheaper all-in than offshore base-taker fees≈0.83–1.24 bps a side on a $250 name, against ≈4.5–5.5 bps offshore, before the offshore spread.

Onboarding: "An NDA, a conformance slot, a switch"

Four steps: an NDA, UAT, internal approval, enable. "There is no product contract to sign", because "exchange products are never negotiated".

There are four starting points:

  • an FCM or OCC clearing member activating MIAX Futures privileges under the May 20, 2026 participant-exchange arrangement;
  • a market-making firm;
  • a broker-dealer or wealth platform filing a Form 7-R;
  • a retail broker coming in through a carrying FCM.

Access needs nothing new: "Nobody needs MIAX Futures Clearing House membership for this class — ever."

Sources: Launch Partner Brief v2.4 (Sept 2026) pp1–23; Fact Sheet (2026); Contract Specifications (2026)

The member build: four inputs, four booking rules, one screen, one report line

The member build is done once and is the same for every asset class: "every future perpetual class rides it unchanged." The only recurring per-name clearing item is OCC's margin-data entry. "Your firm never computes a rate, never recalculates an index, and never encounters a missing row."

4 · 4 · 1 · 1inputs, booking rules, screen, report lineThe whole member-side build, identical at every firm and reused by every later class.

The four inputs

# Input What it carries Detail
1 Feed message 18 The Funding Rate record Predicted rate every 5 s (type 'P'); settled print at :00 (type 'S'). Number 18 is "proposed"
2 Funding file onyx_financials_funding_rate_YYYYMMDD.csv Nine columns, rewritten whole at each lock, "never appended"; fetched, not sent
3 OCC DDS "Funding Adjustment" line "The money" Per OCC account: securities-customer, segregated futures, house
4 Instrument definitions Type 'P' plus no-expiry sentinels 999912; 65535 (= June 6, 2149) on feeds; 9999-12-31 in text; 99991231 at OCC

The file's nine columns run:

  1. product code
  2. Instrument ID
  3. lock time
  4. rate
  5. Mark at lock
  6. Index at lock
  7. Index status
  8. Funding Status
  9. the cycle's running per-contract total

The sentinels are placeholder far-future dates. A no-expiry contract still has to fill date fields built for expiring ones, and a zero "decodes to 1970 and reads as expired".

The four booking rules

  1. "Compute and show each lock as unrealized funding, and count it in equity." The accrual is rate × Mark-at-lock × contracts as of the lock × 100, at full precision. "Never round a per-lock accrual."
  2. "Positions as of the lock time govern." The position at :00 wears the hour. Round trips inside the hour accrue nothing. A trade after the 4 p.m. closing lock rolls to the next cycle.
  3. "A locked row is final." There is no bust and no true-up. A paused hour is rate 0, and no row is ever missing. "Take the count from the cycle, never from a constant": 23 rows normally, 20 on an early close, 19 in a name's first cycle.
  4. "Post once, at the wire." One labeled "Funding Adjustment" cash line, posted inside the daily variation payment and itemized beside variation.

One record, three deliveries, one tie-out

Each live hour the Exchange locks one record per name: lock time, rate, Mark, Index, Index status and Funding Status, "final at :00, no bust." The record goes out three ways: on the feed, in the file, and as a furnish to OCC.

Once a day, OCC computes each member's funding as start-of-cycle position × the cycle's per-contract total, plus each trade × the total of the locks after it. It checks that each name's pool nets to zero, rounds once and prints the DDS line. The cash rides the next business day's variation wire.

The tie-out is one identity: "Σ customer cent-rounded postings + your rounding adjustment = your DDS Funding Adjustment line with its sign reversed."

"If the tie-out otherwise misses, a position is off somewhere — never the rate."— Launch Partner Brief, Sept 2026

The brief spells out three edge cases:

  • A name that fails OCC's zero-sum check after correction is held and paid later. "Only the settlement date moves."
  • Give-ups get "one replay, after finality." A trade executed at 10:14 a.m. and given up at 3:00 p.m. bears funding at the carrying firm from the 11:00 lock. "Accrued funding is derived state", so no balance ever moves between firms.
  • A flat account "can carry a Funding Adjustment line for hours it owned earlier in the cycle."

Variation settles in the same wire. The cycle and the variation window "cover the same clock … neither overlap nor leave a gap."

Sources: UTS Annex C §C.1–C.4 (rev. Sept 30, 2026); Launch Partner Brief v2.4 pp8–11 (Sept 2026); Connamara Integration Brief pp6–7 (Sept 2026)

The clearing-member annex

Annex C turns the four rules into a build for each role. Most roles build far less than the full stack.

Role Account door Portfolio margin What it builds
OCC clearing member, no customers House — The DDS line only
Full FCM + full BD Either (genuine election) Yes Full stack
BD adding SFPs by Form 7-R Securities, mandatory Yes Full stack
Futures broker adding SFPs by Form BD-N Futures, mandatory "Structurally foreclosed" Full stack
Prime broker Either Yes As full FCM/BD
Market maker / Security Futures Dealer Outside the customer regime — Feed handler, give-in back-fill, close-only control
Prop trading firm — — As market maker
Introducing broker; retail app ("Usually an IB or introducing BD") Inherits the carrier's — The display set only

Registration is by notice, "not full dual registration". A security future is both a security and a futures contract, so the firm handling customer orders registers with both the SEC and the CFTC.

  • A broker-dealer adds the futures side on Form 7-R with NFA, effective on filing. It carries the perp in a securities account, with SIPC protection and Rule 15c3-3.
  • An FCM adds the securities side on Form BD-N. It carries the perp in a futures account, under CEA §4d segregation and without SIPC.

Before a customer's first order the firm needs:

  • account approval;
  • the joint risk disclosure plus a supplement for "no-expiration, funding-bearing security futures" (customer sales wait for FINRA and NFA to publish it);
  • an account-election disclosure.

The onboarding checklist is the same at every firm:

  • a message-18 feed handler;
  • a funding-file pickup job;
  • the DDS line routed to a funding sub-ledger;
  • a lock-time position snapshot;
  • give-in back-fill;
  • a close-only order-entry control;
  • statement templates;
  • disclosure and account approval;
  • large-trader reporting;
  • the customer display;
  • OCC intake on Ovation ("no ENCORE");
  • connectivity to the perp's own Onyx instance;
  • extended-hours clearing;
  • UAT on one script.

One UAT check reads: "a 65535 feed date read as June 6, 2149 — never as expired, with no 32-bit time conversion overflowing." FIX order entry and drop copy are unchanged, and Onyx risk protections are "Configuration, not build". In the annex's words: "nothing waits on anything novel from your vendors: one feed message, one file, one report line."

Corporate actions run in four lanes. "One classifier, four lanes: declared cash → A; a whole-number ratio → B; anything else with value or a re-basing → C where the name survives with a consolidated NBBO, D where it does not."

  • Lane A cuts the prior settlement price by the dividend.
  • Lane B multiplies counts and divides the price.
  • Lane C settles the contract and relists fresh.
  • Lane D settles through a four-tier price waterfall.

"No position conversion ever hits your books." The pitfall the annex flags: "a 0.50 step read against a stale cached prior is a phantom −1.5%." Close-only periods are the member's to enforce, since "The matching engine cannot distinguish opening from closing."

One tax question is open: is the hourly Funding Adjustment part of the contract's gain or loss, or a separate item, and where does it go on Form 1099-B? Each carrying firm's tax team must settle it before launch.

Sources: UTS Annex C §C.0–C.8 (rev. Sept 30, 2026); Launch Partner Brief v2.4 pp12–23 (Sept 2026); Connamara Integration Brief pp17–18 (Sept 2026)

Margin, as customers meet it

The Exchange sets no margin level of its own. The federal 15% (initial and maintenance) is a floor. What a customer posts depends on the account door the firm uses.

Rung Account What the customer posts Notes
1 Futures account SPAN on OCC scan ranges, floored at 15% No offset against stock or options
2 Securities, strategy-based 20% (FINRA 4210(f)(10)) The Exchange asks FINRA to conform to 15%
3 Securities, portfolio margin Near 15%, floor $37.50 a contract One computation across perp, stock and options
4 Market maker (Security Futures Dealer) Outside the customer regime From SEC approval of Rule 3.3.2(e)

Funding is never an input to the requirement, but it counts in equity. "A persistent negative carry erodes equity toward a house call."

One NVDAP contract (the perp on NVIDIA), worked through:

  • Notional: at a $180.00 Mark, 100 × $180 = $18,000.
  • Strategy-based margin: 20% = $3,600, or 5× leverage.
  • Portfolio margin: the "illustrative unhedged risk-scan result" is $2,700, or 6.7×. That is exactly 15%.
  • Equity on a long bought at $178.50: the price gain is +$150.00 and five locks at $0.756 cost −$3.78, so equity is +$146.22.

OCC margins the clearing member separately: "STANS on the net portfolio, SPAN gross for a segregated futures account — never on a customer's screen."

$2,700margin on one NVDAP contract at a $180 Mark (6.7×)The portfolio-margin risk scan, equal to 15% of $18,000 notional. Strategy-based margin is $3,600 (5×).

Portfolio margin is a ten-step path. On the FINRA side, it needs:

  • approval from the firm's designated examining authority;
  • customer approvals;
  • a signed disclosure;
  • a dedicated account.

Portfolio-margin accounts under $5 million have carried an intraday-margin obligation since June 4, 2026. On the OCC side it needs:

  • a DDS output form;
  • an SFTP or IBM MQ link;
  • OCC's nightly risk data (ten pre-computed P&L points per contract);
  • class-group mapping and certification;
  • ten days' notice before an account-type change.

"Nothing in that table comes to the Exchange."

Enforcement is the firm's. "Margin enforcement is yours, on published parameters." There is no insurance fund and no auto-deleveraging. OCC's default waterfall stands behind every contract.

The same $2,700 carries two labels: a portfolio-margin risk scan in the Robinhood Brief, and the futures-account result (when OCC's scan is at or below 15%) in the Connamara brief's task 08. The numbers agree; the labels could mislead a customer.
FINRA's 20% in securities accounts stands until FINRA amends it. The Exchange's request to cut it to 15% is an ask, not a result.

Sources: Launch Partner Brief v2.4 pp16–18 (Sept 2026); UTS Annex C §C.5 (rev. Sept 30, 2026); Robinhood Brief p11 (Sept 2026); Connamara Integration Brief p20 (Sept 2026)

The Market Maker Program — the funding print as a borrow rate

The pitch to market makers is a new market, not leverage: "two markets in one instrument: the delta, and the funding print — an exchange-published, per-name financing rate, new to U.S. equities." The brief is titled "A new tape in U.S. equities."

Why the print is a borrow rate

Makers' quotes are two of the Mark's three inputs, sampled about 720 times an hour. So makers set both the reference price and the financing print. "Fair value has a second term": the premium settles at the name's net financing spread.

  • Easy-to-borrow names trade near flat.
  • Hard-to-borrow names trade at a discount with negative funding, so shorts pay and longs collect.

Today borrow is "a phone-call market" run through prime brokers: "bilateral, opaque, different for every account". Short interest is reported twice a month. On the perp, a short needs "no locate, no borrow desk, no recall".

A name's financing now prints in four places:

  1. stock loan, bilateral;
  2. the options surface, implied;
  3. CME's dated strip, as term rates from July 27;
  4. the funding rate, published hourly.

"Where prints diverge, a market exists."

The July 27 CME memo had the direction backwards for a hard-to-borrow name: it said the print "pays the short the scarcity price". The September 18 erratum corrects it: "at a discount the short pays and the long receives."

The flywheel is six steps:

  1. Every flow arrives in one book.
  2. Each arrival tightens it.
  3. A tighter book gives a truer print.
  4. A truer print pulls the next arrival.
  5. Depth compounds.
  6. The book "cannot be dislodged by a copy".

With no strike grid, no expiry strip and no roll, liquidity "cannot fragment".

"The venue that prints a name's financing owns the name's flow — and the firms that make the book set the print."— Market Maker Brief, Sept 2026

Economics: forty cents a side

The maker fee is $0.40 a side, about 27% of the regular $1.50 and 40% of a $1.00 tick. Quoting costs about 0.12 bps a side at the realized notional of roughly $33,600 a contract, or 0.16 bps on a $250 name. Pass-throughs add about $0.07–$0.10 a side.

There are no rebates: "None, at launch, by design … not a taker-funded subsidy." "No ladders, no tokens, no requalification." Membership is a separate thing ("the Preferred Rate Program renamed on September 18, 2026") and does not earn the maker rate by itself.

$0.40market-maker fee per sideAbout 27% of the $1.50 regular fee; ≈0.12 bps of notional to quote; no rebates.

The quoting standard

The recommended terms follow MIAX's own B500 program (certified June 16, 2026). The final terms are MIAX's to set.

Window Two-sided time Size Width
The stock's regular hours ≥ 90% ≥ 5 contracts a side Greater of $0.20 or 1.5× the stock's spread
Rest of the session ≥ 50% ≥ 1 contract Same

Performance is measured monthly per name, with halted periods excluded. A maker that falls short two months running loses the rate and the designation in that name. The standard is published, because it doubles as the Security Futures Dealer holding-out standard, "the form the SEC approved for OneChicago's makers". Incentives can stay confidential. Approval attaches to the class and carries to successor names.

Seat, build and risk

The seat. Rule 2.1.9, amended June 2026, lets MIAX run an incentive program "for any contract or product". A maker registers as a Security Futures Dealer in three steps:

  1. Register as an SEC dealer or a CFTC floor trader.
  2. File the Exchange's dealer form, with three undertakings.
  3. Quote, and keep records.

The margin exclusion runs from SEC approval of Rule 3.3.2(e). Until then, a maker clearing through another firm posts the customer floor.

The build. "Order entry unchanged. One message to parse. One conformance test." The shortest path is "An options market maker at OCC today", and "no adjudication reopens a locked rate or a Daily Settlement Price".

The risk. "Funding is an uncapped pass-through, by design". That is "real, unbounded carry risk", though the hourly average dilutes any one sample to about 1/720th.

The cohort runs MIAX's own member makers first and offshore perp trading firms second. Four flows feed the book:

  • Robinhood retail;
  • carry desks (long stock, short perp);
  • options desks needing a hedge that never rolls;
  • portfolio-margin offsets.

The ask is the names a maker would make, a contact and the NDA. The brief closes: "The book is new. It is yours to make."

Sources: Market Maker Brief (Sept 2026); Room Update (Sept 18, 2026) erratum; Room Update (Sept 28, 2026) p6; How to Win pp8, 12 (Sept 2026)

Robinhood: the perp, in the app

Robinhood is the worked example because it is "the hardest ordinary case". It is a carrying and clearing broker with an introducing broker in front, a retail front end with its own design system, and portfolio margin across three products on one balance. "Every other firm is a subset of it." The pitch puts the perp in the customer's existing securities account, beside the stock and its options: "one balance for the three."

Robinhood's own record

Date Who What they said or did
Dec 2023 24 Hour Market "the only US retail brokerage to offer 24/5 trading of single name stocks"
May 21, 2025 Robinhood Derivatives letter to the CFTC "We believe that 24/7 trading is and should be the future of our capital markets, including perpetual futures."
Jan 21, 2026 JB Mackenzie, MIAXdx close "exploring future partnership opportunities with a technology driven leader in the exchange industry"
Feb 10, 2026 Vlad Tenev, Q4 call Perps outside the U.S.: "we're getting really good feedback"

Sep 29, 2026 HOOD Summit '26 Crypto perps "right inside the Robinhood app": eight assets, "Offered by Robinhood Derivatives through Bitstamp"

The parties

"MIAX's parent and Robinhood own an exchange together."

  • May 2023: LedgerX, FTX's U.S. derivatives exchange, went to MIAX from the FTX estate and became MIAXdx.
  • January 2026: Miami International Holdings sold 90% of it to a venture Robinhood leads with Susquehanna, and kept 10%. The venture is now Rothera, a CFTC-registered exchange and clearinghouse with a 24/7 order book.

Robinhood's stock trades clear at NSCC and its options at OCC. MIAX Futures became an OCC participant exchange on May 20, 2026.

The seat: two notices, one door

Two Robinhood entities file Form 7-R with NFA, "each effective on filing":

  • Robinhood Securities, LLC, the carrying broker and an OCC clearing member, notice-registers as an FCM.
  • Robinhood Financial LLC, the introducing broker, notice-registers as an IB.

The rule then fixes the securities account, with SIPC and Rule 15c3-3 protection. The futures door through Robinhood Derivatives, LLC "stays open and is not the one used here, because it would split the customer's balance in two."

NFA's assessment does not apply in this setup. Large-trader reports reuse "the Part 17 reporting Robinhood Derivatives already runs." An earlier two-door, three-notice draft was superseded by September 28.

The four screens

The brief shows four screens, all at 9:41, captioned "ILLUSTRATIVE VALUES. THE DESIGN SYSTEM IS ROBINHOOD'S." The placement: "On the stock's page, Trade opens one more pill — Trade Perps — beside Trade Options … and Home lists the position in its own row, as it lists held options."

The arithmetic holds:

  • 18,000 ÷ 2,700 = 6.67×
  • 2,700 + 1.50 + 0.02 = 2,701.52
  • 5 × $0.756 = $3.78
  • 150.00 − 3.78 = 146.22
−$0.76funding per lock on one long NVDAP contract0.000042 × $180.00 × 1 × 100 = $0.756. A short sees +$0.76.

Robinhood's part

Robinhood's screen has five things on it:

  1. The price, which is the Mark.
  2. The rate, ticking every 5 s and freezing at :00.
  3. The accrual: equity = cash + unrealized P&L + unrealized funding.
  4. The cash line: "Funding Adjustment", once a business day.
  5. The banners: account, ex-date step-down, split, closing-only, terminal notice.

"Robinhood does not compute a rate or an index." On money: "Robinhood's commission rides on top of the exchange fee. The product is a new transaction line — margined, funding-bearing, commissionable — in accounts already held." The all-in cost on the NVDAP example is 1.15–1.72 bps a side.

Next comes "An NDA, two notices, a screen, a switch". After that, "every later name and every later class is a product-list election."

"Robinhood's part is the UI — five things a customer looks at — and the booking beneath it: four rules and one tie-out."— Robinhood Brief, Sept 2026
The brief is a proposal, and the room shows no sign that Robinhood agreed. Robinhood is building its own paths: crypto perps through its own FCM and Bitstamp (announced September 29), and a stake in OG.com, whose exchange filed perps on ten stocks on September 24.

Sources: Robinhood Brief (Sept 2026 edition) pp1–14; Connamara Integration Brief Part 09 (Sept 2026); Room Update (Sept 28, 2026) pp16–17

The display standard: seventeen items, the same everywhere

"One screen" means fixed display semantics, not a shared app: "The design system is the firm's. The semantics are not: the same seventeen items, the same labels, the same arithmetic on every screen in the street, so that a customer who reads the public funding history can recompute every posting on every platform."

# Item R/O Rule
1 Mark Price R "never the last trade alone, never the Index"
2 Index O "Index (published midpoint)"
3 Current funding rate R Per hour, live or predicted
4 Previous settled rate and history R —
5 Annualized rate O Only if labeled: hourly × 23 × 252. "Never × 24 × 365"; never the headline
6 Countdown to next lock O Locks at :00, 22:00 through 20:00
7 Unrealized funding, per position and in total R —
8 Account equation R equity = cash + unrealized P&L + unrealized funding
9 Daily cash line R "two lines, never one blended number"
10 Corporate-action display R Step-down, split, run-down banner
11 Closing-only banner R Blocks new positions
12 Terminal settlement notice R Final price, cash date, successor
13 Fees and pass-throughs O Never inside the funding line
14 Risk disclosure R —
15 Account type and change notice R —
16 Margin basis as one portfolio computation R (PM firms) Never a per-position percentage
17 Dedicated PM account identification R (PM firms) —

One more rule completes it: "a partially halted hour still settles a full hour of funding, computed only from the samples taken while the underlying was live." On the annualization rule, the illustrative +0.000042 an hour × 23 × 252 comes to about 24.3% a year. That is derived arithmetic for this chapter; no document states it.

Sources: UTS Annex C §C.6 (rev. Sept 30, 2026); Connamara Integration Brief pp8–10 (Sept 2026); Street Build memo (Sept 16, 2026)

Connamara and the Street Build option

The Street Build option would replace "thirty separate projects" with one outside engineering team. That team would carry one reference implementation from firm to firm, test it against one script pack and sign it off against one checklist. The decision is MIAX's, and "the launch waits on no outside team."

How it arose. Connamara Systems approached AP. In its own words, it is "co-authors and official maintainers of QuickFIX; custom software for capital markets since 1998", working fully remote. AP prepared a 25-page Integration Brief for Daniel Curry, Connamara's Chief Revenue Officer: "MIAX Futures has not engaged an integration partner; this is what the work would be if it does." It carries no deal terms and no rates. With one team, the build "stops being a project at each firm and becomes a fitting".

Party What it does What it builds
MIAX Futures (Onyx) Samples, locks, publishes, furnishes to OCC Engine, message, file, instrument record, corporate-action lanes
OCC (Ovation) Takes in rows, computes with a zero-sum check, prints the DDS line Three new pieces, an agreement amendment, one rule filing
Clearing member / FCM Books and carries Annex C
Broker / wealth platform Shows Display set and statements
Market maker Quotes Feeds, risk configuration, close-only control
Vendor / OMS / ISV Parses Parser and booking module, once

"The team's work sits in the last four rows."

The engagement is six steps per firm:

  1. scope;
  2. the pack, under NDA;
  3. the fitting;
  4. the test-bed run (MIAX's Firm Test Bed and OCC's scenario testing);
  5. sign-off against the Annex C checklist;
  6. the first cycle, with the first wire's tie-out held and filed. Then "The firm owns it".

Firms come in waves: vendors first ("most firms book through a back-office vendor and trade through an ISV"), then launch firms, then primes, then market makers. A clearing member gets the full kit, and "the first proves the build, the second proves the repeat." An introducing broker is the shortest fitting, a prime the largest.

Thirteen tasks, in build order:

  1. Connect
  2. Identify
  3. Read the market
  4. Take the lock
  5. Pick up the file
  6. Book ("sample cycle books to the cent")
  7. Settle and tie out ("first three wires tie out with no unexplained residual")
  8. Margin
  9. Open the door
  10. Show and state
  11. Handle corporate actions
  12. Test and certify
  13. Go live, watching two numbers: row count per cycle and tie-out residual

The kit is built once and owned by each firm after go-live. It holds:

  • parsers;
  • file pickup;
  • lock-time snapshot;
  • booking module;
  • tie-out job;
  • display and statement templates;
  • alert consumer;
  • identity mapper;
  • an optional portfolio-margin adapter;
  • a script pack;
  • an ops runbook.

Corrections carry forward to the next firm, "never back into a live firm without that firm's change control."

Twelve switches (open settings) remain.

  • Six are the Exchange's: the message number and release; per-name price bounds; the SIP subscriber entity; the no-band value; the codes for GOOGL and BRK.B; the cancel-reason strings.
  • Six are OCC's: the DDS layout and cut-off; identifiers through corporate actions; execution time through a give-up; sentinel acceptance; scan-class dates; portfolio-margin class-group mapping.

Not switches: "four rules, tie-out identity, sign convention, cycle definition, display semantics."

The decision. If MIAX says yes, member services introduces the team and the switches are answered once. If it says no, each firm builds from Annex C and the Runbooks' member chapter.

The idea has a history. In the GLX phase (May 2026), Saheb proposed buying Connamara outright as "GLX Tech", for roughly $40–60M of acqui-hire consideration in the MIAX transaction draft, with a 12-component "proliferation playbook" for taking any broker live. The licensor model cut Connamara to "a vendor doc, not a counterparty". In AP it is an option for MIAX to sponsor.

"The first firm is the one whose stack the team knows best; the second is the one that proves the repeat."— Connamara Integration Brief, Sept 2026

Sources: Street Build memo (Sept 16, conformed Sept 28, 2026); Connamara Integration Brief (Sept 2026); Room Update (Sept 30, 2026) p6; GLX Connamara memo, Connamara.pdf, MIAX.pdf (May 2026)

The public site

The public face belongs to the Exchange. AP supplies the rules, and MIAX does the design.

Naming. Product name, branding, symbology and framing are at the Exchange's sole discretion (The Deal ¶6(d)). "The 'American Perpetual' name appears in a product's name only if the Exchange so elects." Product pages and principal materials carry the credit line "American Perpetual LLC — originator and licensor" and a verbatim disclaimer.

Notices and data. There are two notice rails:

  • Corporate Action Alerts, in four pre-written kinds, plus a Listing Alert for "the inaugural 101 together";
  • numbered Regulatory Circulars, carrying methodology, calendars and the filing record.

Each perp page charts Mark and Index, plus the hourly funding rate, predicted and settled. "No quotation fields from the SIP are redistributed anywhere on the surface — midpoint and status only."

The existing daily files gain perp rows with unchanged layouts. Maturity shows "PERP" or 9999-12-31, and the exercise and delivery columns are always zero. Funding history is free, and DoM feed fees are waived through December 31, 2027.

Copy rules.

  • Funding is "a rate applied per hour during the live session," never annualized in a headline.
  • Long or short is stated "with the matching loss in the same breath".
  • Margin copy is "never an exchange leverage figure". The Robinhood screens' "Leverage 6.7x" is the firm's own number, not the Exchange's.
  • Tax copy carries "no 60/40 claim — that is an index-products claim".
  • The product is "new, never first or only".
  • Explainers draw on the spec section "Versus the crypto perps — what this design deliberately doesn't carry": no insurance fund, no auto-deleveraging, no wallet collateral.

The Fact Sheet heads "EQUITY PERPETUAL FUTURES — A new perpetual future on a U.S. equity." Its stat strip reads: 100 shares · Hourly funding, history public · 23×5 while the tape is live · $0.01 tick, $1.00 per contract.

Sources: UTS Annex D (rev. Sept 30, 2026); Fact Sheet (2026); How to Win Part 04 (Sept 2026)

CME's July 27 relaunch and AP's answer

CME proved that U.S. single-stock futures have demand again. It did so with an expiring contract that runs on an exemption. AP's answer is that the architecture decides the category, not the launch date.

What CME launched on July 27, 2026:

  • dated single-stock futures: 55 standard (100-share) and 22 Micro (10-share) contracts;
  • 50+ mega-cap names, plus "a SpaceX headline listing";
  • quarterly contracts, two expiries listed;
  • about 23 hours on Globex;
  • futures accounts only at CME Clearing, with no offsets beyond calendar spreads;
  • no adjustment for ordinary dividends.

Federal rules point a cash-settled security future at the opening price. CME settles to the close by exemption, under an SEC conditional §36 order (Release 34-105882, July 15, 2026) and CFTC relief effective July 16. The conditions are a market cap of $100B+, $450M+ traded a day and a tenor of at most nine months. Hence "fifty mega-caps and two quarterlies", and a product that "cannot easily go down-cap".

55 + 22CME's standard and Micro dated contracts, July 27, 202650+ mega-caps, quarterly expiries, futures accounts only. AP's memo went to MIAX the same day.

AP's same-day memo to MIAX Products Leadership gave six answers:

  1. OCC on its newest rails, with the full clearing-member network.
  2. The options complex at the same clearinghouse, through portfolio margin.
  3. The funding rate as a live borrow market, "the cleanest short in U.S. equities".
  4. One ticker, so liquidity does not fractionate.
  5. Dated contracts are the wrong architecture for cash settlement; OneChicago failed with them (2002–2020).
  6. Dividends handled by rule: "the NoDivRisk construction, inherited and completed".
Axis Perpetual (MIAX · OCC) Dated relaunch (CME)
Structure No expiry; one book per name Quarterly; four rolls a year
Financing Printed hourly Implied in the basis
Dividends Ex-date factor, by rule Ordinary dividends not adjusted
Clearing OCC beside options; portfolio-margin path CME Clearing; futures account only
Hours SIP-anchored, Sun 9 p.m.–Fri 8 p.m. ET Venue clock, Sun 6 p.m.–Fri 5 p.m. ET
For the maker One book A strip

Hours parity arrives with the SIP's 23×5 production on December 6, 2026, announced July 7.

The compounding plan has four parts:

  • wire the funding line into retail broker-dealers, primes and principal trading firms first;
  • out-list CME at one Schedule entry per name;
  • make §871(m) withholding turnkey (base = factor × 100 × contracts). Section 871(m) is the U.S. withholding on dividend-equivalent payments to non-U.S. holders;
  • cite global demand. South Korea's July 2026 leverage unwind margin-called "over a million" retail accounts, per the memo. How to Win says 1.2 million, with more than 300,000 liquidated.

The prize is "the financing layer of U.S. equities". The memo places it in a line with NYSE for equities, CME for rates, Cboe for volatility and ICE for energy. "Products change; infrastructure compounds."

A legal front is open. How to Win records a pending CME suit asking a court to declare funding-rate perpetuals swaps, and says the CFTC moved to dismiss. AP's Harmonization Submission "cites the pending CME suit by its docket caption".

"On being second: their head start is denominated in an asset that expires."— Market Maker Brief, Sept 2026
"Their product exists by exemption; ours is drafted to the rule's own branches, with no relief sought."— CME Launch memo, July 27, 2026

Sources: CME Launch memo (July 27, conformed Sept 30, 2026); Market Maker Brief p6 (Sept 2026); Room Update (Sept 18, 2026) erratum; How to Win pp4–7 (Sept 2026)

On fees:
